Feature gates: ExperimentalDatalayerFeatureGate is now a deprecated no-op ('data layer is now enabled by default'); EnableLegacyMetricsFeatureGate falls back to the pre-datalayer backend/metrics polling path. Config/DataSourceConfig (v1.3.0) wraps a fwkdl.DataSource plugin plus its Extractors, supporting both poll-based and event-driven (notification) sources — differentiated at setup, which is the extension point most likely portable to non-pod backends since Collector.Start requires only a Ticker, an Endpoint, and a slice of fwkdl.PollingDataSource (no direct k8s dependency at that call site). `evergreen` (1236 words)
- [[datalayer-package-sigsk8siogateway-api-inference-extensionpkgeppframeworkinterfa]] — datalayer package - sigs.k8s.io/gateway-api-inference-extension/pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer - Go Packages — godoc for pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer (the abstract interface package underlying pkg/epp/datalayer's Runtime; discovered via cross-reference from the datalayer package). This is the single most direct answer to whether the data layer assumes Kubernetes pods: 'type EndpointMetadata struct { NamespacedName types.NamespacedName; PodName string; Address string; MetricsHost string; Labels map[string]string }' with doc comment 'EndpointMetadata represents the relevant Kubernetes Pod state of an inference server.' — the concrete metadata struct is Pod-shaped by definition (NamespacedName + PodName + Labels), not a generic backend descriptor. The Endpoint interface itself is more abstract (embeds fmt.Stringer, EndpointMetaState, EndpointMetricsState — i.e. GetMetadata/UpdateMetadata/GetAttributes/GetMetrics/UpdateMetrics), and ModelServer is 'an implementation of the Endpoint interface' built via NewEndpoint(meta *EndpointMetadata, metrics *Metrics). Two DataSource extension points differ sharply in k8s-coupling: PollingDataSource ('Poll(ctx, ep Endpoint) (any, error)') is generic — it only needs an Endpoint and returns arbitrary polled data, with no direct k8s dependency at the interface level, making it the most portable extension point for a non-k8s backend. NotificationSource, by contrast, is explicitly built around Kubernetes watches: 'NotificationSource is an event-driven DataSource for a single k8s GVK. The framework core owns the k8s notification mechanisms (e.g., watches, caches, informers) and calls the source's Notify on events'; EventType constants are literally 'EventAddOrUpdate is fired when a k8s object is created or updated' / 'EventDelete is fired when a k8s object is deleted'; NotificationEvent.Object is a *unstructured.Unstructured (raw k8s object). EndpointSource/EndpointExtractor (added v1.5.0) sit on top of endpoint lifecycle events dispatched by the Runtime, decoupled from the raw k8s watch mechanism but still keyed on the Pod-shaped EndpointMetadata. Metrics struct (ActiveModels, WaitingModels, MaxActiveModels, RunningRequestsSize, WaitingQueueSize, KVCacheUsagePercent, KvCacheMaxTokenCapacity, CacheBlockSize, CacheNumBlocks, UpdateTime) is itself backend-agnostic — no k8s or pod fields — so a custom PollingDataSource could in principle populate a real Metrics object for a non-pod backend, but would still have to synthesize a valid EndpointMetadata (with placeholder NamespacedName/PodName) to satisfy the type. `evergreen` (1730 words)

- [[feat-add-pluggable-discoveryplugin-interface-by-ezrasilvera-pull-request-908-llm]] — feat: Add pluggable DiscoveryPlugin interface by ezrasilvera · Pull Request #908 · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE, highest relevance to the research query. Merged PR #908 (llm-d/llm-d-router), opened Apr 28 2026 by Ezra Silvera (IBM), merged May 3 2026 by ahg-g at commit 0463fa5. This PR defines the exact extension point for adding non-Kubernetes backends to the llm-d router. Full code-review thread captured verbatim.

THE INTERFACE (final form, after review): originally named DiscoveryPlugin at pkg/epp/framework/interface/discovery/discovery.go, renamed to EndpointDiscovery and physically MOVED to under the datalayer package (pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer/discovery.go) per the author's own design rationale. Implementations must also satisfy fwkplugin.Plugin so they can register in the plugin registry and be selected via EndpointPickerConfig.discovery.pluginRef -- i.e. a custom non-k8s discovery backend is enabled purely by config, no fork of the router needed once the plugin is registered. Core method: Start(ctx context.Context, notifier Notifier) error -- expected to enumerate all known endpoints via notifier.Upsert on startup (loosened during review from a hard requirement to a guideline) and then block, continuing to watch for changes until ctx is cancelled or a fatal error occurs; the PR explicitly leaves concurrency management (whether Start spawns its own goroutine) to the plugin's discretion, documented via a code comment added during review. The Notifier interface exposes Upsert(endpoint *fwkdl.EndpointMetadata) and Delete(id types.NamespacedName); review comments established the notifier is NOT goroutine-safe and plugin implementations MUST preserve event ordering (e.g. an Upsert followed by a Delete for the same endpoint must arrive in that order or the endpoint is incorrectly left in the datastore).

*** THE SINGLE MOST DIRECTLY RESPONSIVE QUOTE TO THE RESEARCH QUERY ***, from the PR author (Ezra Silvera) explaining why Discovery must be a separate, pluggable component from the datalayer: "We aim to also support non k8s in the future so we need to keep the datalayer clean: we want K8s, file, Slurm, and Ray discovery all as plugins. If discovery lives inside the datalayer, the datalayer gets contaminated with infrastructure-specific code (ctrl.Manager, fsnotify, etc.), which defeats the whole point of the abstraction." This CONFIRMS that (a) the llm-d-router maintainers designed the discovery abstraction specifically to be backend-agnostic across Kubernetes, flat files, Slurm, AND Ray, and (b) Kubernetes-specific machinery (ctrl.Manager, i.e. controller-runtime) lives ONLY inside the k8s discovery plugin implementation, not in the shared datalayer/notifier code path -- meaning a from-scratch non-k8s DiscoveryPlugin implementation does not need to touch or depend on any Kubernetes client libraries.

The author also articulated the architectural boundary precisely: "Discovery is upstream of the datalayer, not a peer, the datalayer only activates per endpoint AFTER discovery creates it... Discovery answers 'which endpoints exist?' [while] the datalayer answers 'what is the state of known endpoints?' (metrics, KV cache, queue depth)" -- i.e. a custom backend integration needs a Discovery plugin (topology/existence) and can reuse the existing datalayer machinery (health/metrics polling) unmodified, PROVIDED the backend exposes compatible metrics endpoints.

One open design question flagged but explicitly NOT resolved in this PR: a reviewer asked "I wonder if this is not too k8s centric and we should include an explicit UniqueID in the endpoint metadata?" referring to the use of Kubernetes' types.NamespacedName as the endpoint identifier type in Delete(id types.NamespacedName) -- the author agreed this is a real gap ("several other places using NamespacedName and we should switch to ID") but deferred it as out of scope, meaning as of this PR the endpoint identifier type itself still carries a k8s-shaped type even though the interface is otherwise backend-agnostic; downstream PRs (e.g. the file-discovery series #1080/#1081) may or may not have resolved this -- worth checking.

Linked follow-on/related work visible in the PR thread: issue #910 "Refactor the Pod watching to fit the Discovery plugin" (making the existing Kubernetes pod-watch conform to the new interface), issue #911 "Implement a File discovery plugin", and a related closed PR #1007 "Discovery Plugins: K8s and File-based Endpoint Discovery". `evergreen` (2371 words)

- [[feat-replay-buffer-consumer-for-kv-event-index-recovery-by-rishabhsaini-pull-r-2]] — feat: replay buffer consumer for KV event index recovery by RishabhSaini · Pull Request #1887 · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PR #1887 (llm-d/llm-d-router, merged Aug 6, 2026 by vMaroon) is the actual, currently-merged fix that gives llm-d's KV-cache index disaster recovery via vLLM's KV-event replay buffer, closing out issue #372's root cause. The ZMQ subscriber (pkg/kvevents/zmq_subscriber.go) connects to vLLM's ROUTER socket via a DEALER and: (1) proactively replays on connect so the index rebuilds immediately without waiting for live traffic; (2) detects sequence gaps (seq > lastSeq+1) and requests replay starting at lastSeq+1; (3) tracks DUAL watermarks -- lastSeq (what's been indexed) vs lastLiveSeq (what's arrived on the wire) -- specifically so replay overlap cannot inflate the kvevents dedup reference counts described in the kvevents package docs; (4) applies a strict event gate so that after replay, live events with seq<=lastSeq are dropped before reaching AddTask; (5) detects sequence RESET (seq < lastLiveSeq, e.g. vLLM pod restarted but kept the same IP so EnsureSubscriber no-ops) and clears the index and dedup state via the pool queue; (6) backs off 30s on failed replay only, with success clearing failure state immediately; (7) uses DEALER instead of REQ because REQ can only receive one reply per multi-message replay. EnsureSubscriber's signature grew sourceEndpoint and replayEndpoint parameters (a breaking change) computed from PodDiscoveryConfig.ReplaySocketPort. Pre-merge review (Jul 30, 2026) caught real correctness bugs in earlier revisions -- gap replay double-storing an already-buffered event because vLLM's _service_replay resends anything with seq>=start_seq, corrupting the dedup reference count and leaking a block that should have been evicted; and cooldown freezing lastSeq while AddTask kept running, so the eventual retry re-sent (and re-double-stored) the ENTIRE frozen window, not just the missed event. Depends on vllm-project/vllm#45177 (merged) for the topic-frame fix in vLLM's own replay protocol. This PR supersedes both llm-d-kv-cache PR #663 (EmptyBlockHash-only fallback) and PR #691 (the same replay-buffer feature attempted in the pre-migration repo). `evergreen` (2039 words)
- [[feat-replay-buffer-consumer-for-kv-event-index-recovery-by-rishabhsaini-pull-req]] — feat: replay buffer consumer for KV event index recovery by RishabhSaini · Pull Request #691 · llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache · GitHub — PR #691 (llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache, opened Jun 30, 2026) is an earlier attempt at the replay-buffer fix for issue #372: the ZMQ subscriber would connect to vLLM's ROUTER socket via a DEALER on connect/reconnect and proactively replay up to 10,000 buffered event batches to rebuild the KV-block index in seconds, detecting sequence gaps (seq > lastSeq+1) to request replay of missed events, with a 30s cooldown after failed replay only. Depended on vllm-project/vllm#45177 (merged) for the topic-frame fix in vLLM's replay protocol. States plainly the problem being solved: 'After an EPP restart, the KV block index is empty. Without replay, the index only recovers as new requests generate new blocks -- cached prefixes from before the restart are invisible to routing.' This PR was CLOSED on Jul 14, 2026 in favor of the equivalent work landing directly in llm-d-router (PR #1887), consistent with the kvcache/kvblock/kvevents migration out of llm-d-kv-cache. `evergreen` (1246 words)

- [[file-discovery-34-add-filediscovery-plugin-with-yamljson-file-loading-and-fsnoti]] — file-discovery (3/4):  add FileDiscovery plugin with YAML/JSON file loading and fsnotify watch by ezrasilvera · Pull Request #1135 · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE. Merged PR #1135 (llm-d/llm-d-router), "file-discovery (3/4): add FileDiscovery plugin with YAML/JSON file loading and fsnotify watch", opened May 14 2026 by Ezra Silvera, merged May 19 2026 by ahg-g at commit 25b2584. Third of a four-part PR series adding file-based discovery "allowing the EPP to run without Kubernetes"; the author states file-based discovery is "the first phase in re-creating the functionality of #1007" (an earlier closed PR, "Discovery Plugins: K8s and File-based Endpoint Discovery"). This PR implements FileDiscovery, described in the PR body as "the first EndpointDiscovery backend for non-Kubernetes deployments."

BEHAVIOR: FileDiscovery reads a YAML or JSON file listing inference endpoints and drives their lifecycle in the datastore via DiscoveryNotifier. On startup it loads endpoints from the file and emits one Upsert per entry so the datastore is populated before the EPP begins serving traffic. It can optionally watch the file with fsnotify and reconcile additions/removals on every write -- "allowing zero-downtime endpoint updates without restarting the EPP." On reload it diffs the previous snapshot against new file contents and removes stale endpoints. Configured via EndpointPickerConfig.dataLayer.discovery.pluginRef pointing to a plugin instance of type "file-discovery" with "path" and "watchFile" params. NOTE (important caveat): as of this PR the plugin is "fully implemented and tested in isolation but is not yet usable end-to-end" -- the runner wiring, config detection, and runWithFileDiscovery execution path shipped separately in the follow-on PR #1218 (4/4); until #1218 merged, setting dataLayer.discovery.pluginRef in the config had no effect.

CONFIG SCHEMA (evolved during review): the per-endpoint YAML/JSON schema is EndpointEntry{ Name string; Namespace string (optional); Address string; Port string }. A "MetricsHost" field was added, then briefly renamed to "MetricsPort" to let operators point metrics scraping at a different port than inference traffic (motivated by "vLLM running inference on :8000 but /metrics on :9090"), then a reviewer pointed out the EPP "can not scrape and serve on different IPs, only same IP and different port is feasible (and deprecated)" -- the author ultimately DROPPED the metrics-port override entirely, so the final shipped behavior always scrapes Prometheus metrics from the same address:port the endpoint serves inference on, with no way to configure a separate metrics port via the file-discovery plugin as merged.

NOTABLE LIMITATION: a commit titled "file-discovery: validate IPv4 only and reject IPv6 addresses" shows the plugin explicitly rejects IPv6 endpoint addresses -- IPv6 backends cannot be registered via file-discovery as of this PR.

K8S RESIDUE: despite being the non-Kubernetes discovery backend, the internal endpoint-identity tracking still uses Kubernetes' types.NamespacedName as the map key type (`endpoints map[types.NamespacedName]struct{}`, renamed from `current` during review) -- confirming the k8s-shaped identifier gap flagged as unresolved in PR #908 persisted into this implementation. The plugin also depends on "sigs.k8s.io/yaml" for YAML parsing (chosen because it "converts all to json" so json struct tags alone suffice) -- a Kubernetes-ecosystem library, though used here purely as a YAML-to-JSON codec with no live cluster dependency.

RESILIENCE FIXES made during review (both by reviewer nilig/vMaroon, addressed by author): (1) a "partial-Upsert atomicity bug that can leave zombie endpoints in the datastore" during file load; (2) "missing watcher re-add that breaks watchFile mode after atomic renames" -- fixed via a commit "re-attach fsnotify watcher after atomic rename and ConfigMap rotation." That commit message explicitly names ConfigMap rotation as a scenario the plugin must tolerate, implying the file-discovery plugin is also designed to work when the backing file is mounted into a Kubernetes pod from a ConfigMap (which Kubernetes updates via atomic symlink swap) -- i.e. file-discovery is usable BOTH fully outside Kubernetes (e.g. a flat file on a Slurm node) AND inside a Kubernetes pod as an alternative to the native K8s/InferencePool discovery plugin. Additionally, the author changed reload behavior to "skip invalid endpoints on reload instead of aborting the entire load," and added a "README for file-discovery plugin" following the project's plugin-documentation template (issue #917), plus a promised follow-up discovery.md describing the overall discovery-plugin framework.

Package path: pkg/epp/framework/plugins/datalayer/discovery/file/plugin.go (module path is github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router, following the project's earlier rename from llm-d-inference-scheduler). Immediate successor PR referenced in the thread: #1218 "FileDiscovery plugin (4/4) - wiring into the runner" (merged). `evergreen` (3547 words)
- [[fixsidecar-await-prefill-before-dispatching-pd-multi-tier-decode-leg-by-nilig-pu]] — fix(sidecar): await prefill before dispatching PD Multi Tier decode leg by nilig · Pull Request #2451 · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — Merged llm-d-router PR #2451 (by nilig, Aug 19 2026) fixing issue #2438: changes handleP2P (pkg/sidecar/proxy/connector_p2p.go) so the prefill leg runs to completion before the decode leg is dispatched, returning the prefill error verbatim on failure so decode is never dispatched against KV that was never stored. Matches the nixlv2 connector's existing sequential ordering (concurrent dispatch there is opt-in, gated behind MoRIIOParallelDispatch && MoRIIOWriteMode). Confirms the same before/after measurements as issue #2438 (supply delay mean 15.830s->0.002s; consumer load latency mean 14.387s->0.291s on 8 prefill + 8 decode gpt-oss-120b, TP=1, vLLM v0.27.1, 16x H200) and explicitly notes the fix does not eliminate all timeouts -- a producer that already holds requested blocks still supplies nothing regardless of dispatch ordering, tracked as vllm-project/vllm#52808. Test plan confirms decode request count stays zero until prefill releases, then fires exactly once. `evergreen` (1850 words)
- [[fix-harden-header-sanitization-and-handling-logic-by-lukeavandrie-pull-request-2]] — fix: harden header sanitization and handling logic by LukeAVanDrie · Pull Request #2025 · kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension · GitHub — GIE PR #2025 (LukeAVanDrie, merged Dec 19 2025, later cherry-picked into llm-d/llm-d-router Apr 23 2026) hardens ext_proc header handling so a client-supplied x-gateway-destination-endpoint cannot override EPP's own routing decision. Before the fix, generateHeaders()/generateResponseHeaders() did an 'include all headers' passthrough of the incoming request/response header map, so an attacker-set x-gateway-destination-endpoint (and other EPP-owned headers) would ride along untouched. The fix adds pkg/epp/util/request/headers.go IsSystemOwnedHeader(), which checks three blocklists -- InputControlHeaders (flow-fairness-id, objective, model-name-rewrite, subset-filter), OutputInjectionHeaders (destination-endpoint, destination-endpoint-served), and ProtocolHeaders (content-length) -- and silently drops any of these from the passthrough loop, letting only the value EPP itself sets (from reqCtx.TargetEndpoint) survive. PR description: 'Previously, the header generation logic in generateHeaders (Request) and generateResponseHeaders (Response) acted as a transparent passthrough, appending user-supplied headers to the outgoing message... These changes are necessary to prevent ambiguity in downstream proxies (Envoy) and backends.' The added test TestGenerateHeaders_Sanitization directly proves the client-injected value is discarded: it sets metadata.DestinationEndpointKey to '1.1.1.1:666' on the inbound headers and asserts the generated header instead equals reqCtx.TargetEndpoint's real value '1.2.3.4:8080'. `evergreen` (2018 words)
- [[fix-include-topic-frame-in-kv-events-replay-response-by-rishabhsaini-pull-reques]] — fix: include topic frame in KV events replay response by RishabhSaini · Pull Request #45177 · vllm-project/vllm · GitHub — vLLM PR #45177 ('fix: include topic frame in KV events replay response', author RishabhSaini) adds a topic field to replay ROUTER responses so the wire format matches live PUB messages ([topic, seq, payload]), and switches test replay sockets from REQ to DEALER for multi-message replay with strict assertions. - [[fix-index-blocks-with-unknown-parent-using-empty-parent-hash-by-rishabhsaini-pul]] — fix: index blocks with unknown parent using empty parent hash by RishabhSaini · Pull Request #663 · llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache · GitHub — PR #663 (llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache, opened Jun 12, 2026 by RishabhSaini) implements the interim fix described in issue #372: when a BlockStoredEvent references a parent block hash not present in the index (e.g. after a GAIE/EPP restart clears in-memory state), pool.go's processEventBatch now falls back to EmptyBlockHash as the parent and continues processing the child block, instead of silently dropping it via 'continue' -- which previously cascaded to drop all descendant blocks too, since their parents never entered the index. Log level for this case changes from Error to Info since it is now an expected condition during index recovery, not a failure. A Copilot reviewer flagged that the fix treats ALL GetRequestKey errors as 'parent not in index' rather than distinguishing not-found from other failures (e.g. storage/serialization/corruption), risking masked real errors. IMPORTANT CORRECTION: despite being fully implemented and reviewed, this PR was ultimately CLOSED (not merged) on Jul 3, 2026 -- the author's own follow-up commit message states 'Remove pool.go EmptyBlockHash fallback (separate PR #663)', indicating the approach was superseded by the more complete replay-buffer-consumer fix (PR #691) referenced in issue #372's later comments, which rebuilds the full hash chain from vLLM's KV-event replay buffer on subscriber reconnect rather than indexing with a placeholder empty parent hash. `evergreen` (2935 words)
- [[gateway-api-inference-extensiondocsproposals003-model-server-protocolreadmemd-at]] — gateway-api-inference-extension/docs/proposals/003-model-server-protocol/README.md at main · kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension · GitHub — GIE proposal 003, the Model Server Protocol (status: Partially implemented, notes it cannot be as strict since the pluggable EPP architecture landed). This is the exact contract any non-pod backend must satisfy to work with GIE's endpoint picker. Requirements: (1) model server MUST implement OpenAI's Completions and Chat HTTP APIs; (2) model server MUST expose a Prometheus-style metrics endpoint with these metrics (type + semantics fixed, names are flexible) -- TotalQueuedRequests (Gauge, vllm:num_requests_waiting / nv_trt_llm_request_metrics{request_type=waiting} / trtllm_num_requests_waiting / sglang:num_queue_reqs), TotalRunningRequests (Gauge, vllm:num_requests_running / nv_trt_llm_request_metrics{request_type=scheduled} / trtllm_num_requests_running / sglang:num_running_reqs), KVCacheUtilization (Gauge percentage, vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc / nv_trt_llm_kv_cache_block_metrics{kv_cache_block_type=fraction} / trtllm_kv_cache_utilization / sglang:token_usage), and optional BlockSize/NumGPUBlocks labeled gauges (used by prefix-cache scorer, derivable from EPP prefix-plugin config if absent). (3) For LoRA: model servers MUST support serving an adapter named in the request's model field, and MUST expose a vllm:lora_requests_info-style Gauge with labels max_lora, running_lora_adapters, waiting_lora_adapters (comma-separated adapter lists) via the same Prometheus endpoint. (4) Prefix cache reuse (SHOULD, since EPP v0.4.0) for prefix-aware scheduling. Nothing in the protocol references Kubernetes objects directly -- it is purely an HTTP+Prometheus contract, which is the load-bearing fact for building a non-pod backend. `evergreen` (768 words)
- [[gateway-api-inference-extensiondocsproposals004-endpoint-picker-protocol-at-main]] — gateway-api-inference-extension/docs/proposals/004-endpoint-picker-protocol at main · kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension · GitHub — GIE proposal 004, the Endpoint Picker Protocol spec (status: Implemented, versioned to v1.0.0 dated 2025-07-29). Defines the wire contract between a data-plane proxy (e.g. Envoy) and the EPP over Envoy ext_proc, which the EPP MUST implement including streaming mode. Endpoint identity on the wire is purely ip:port strings, not k8s object references: the data plane can restrict the EPP to a subset via filterMetadata key envoy.lb.subset_hint / x-gateway-destination-endpoint-subset (list of ip:port); if unset the EPP selects from the InferencePool selector's endpoint set. The EPP returns its pick via the x-gateway-destination-endpoint HTTP header AND the envoy.lb dynamic_metadata namespace (both required to match; mismatch is undefined behavior), as one or more comma-separated ip:port endpoints — first valid one used sequentially for retries. Constraints: 503 Service Unavailable if the EPP fails to set a destination when no endpoints are ready, 429 Too Many Requests to shed load. A 2026-07-23 amendment (PR #3003, 'clarify EPP endpoint backend scope') scoped the ordered endpoint list to the specific InferencePool backend that produced it: on same-backend retry the data plane continues down that backend's list; on route-level retry/failover to a different InferencePool backend, the data plane MUST invoke that backend's EPP and MUST NOT reuse the previous backend's endpoints, and x-gateway-destination-endpoint-served (health/serving confirmation) must be reported to the EPP tied to whichever backend produced the terminal response. Also specifies gRPC Health Checking Protocol services (liveness, readiness, ext_proc service check) the EPP must expose. `evergreen` (1296 words)
- [[gateway-api-inference-extensiondocsproposals0683-epp-architecture-proposal-at-ma]] — gateway-api-inference-extension/docs/proposals/0683-epp-architecture-proposal at main · kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension · GitHub — Proposal 0683 (author @kfswain, status: Implemented) that restructures the EPP into modular layers: a non-extensible Ext-Proc Server (deviation 'ill-advised' since it must track the well-defined ext_proc protocol precisely) plus four extensible layers — Data Layer, Routing Layer, Flow Controller, Scheduling Layer. The Data Layer is called out as the one layer 'accessed by all the others': it 'consumes and stores the InferencePool/InferenceModel config and the pre-defined Model Server Protocol,' processes model-server-fed data into resource-scarcity-regime hints and autoscaling recommendations, and is explicitly built to be extended — but 'extenders accept that the Model Server Protocol will no longer provide guarantees on portability of a model server out of the box' once extended. This is the architectural seam a router customizer would target to swap in a non-k8s-pod data source. Routing Layer handles per-InferenceModel routing rules (system-prompt injection, RAG callout, request validation) with a deliberately tight API scope to avoid becoming a dumping ground for custom rules. `evergreen` (890 words)

- [[gateway-api-inference-extensionsite-srcguidesstandalonemd-at-91ad0da44f64d41642d]] — gateway-api-inference-extension/site-src/guides/standalone.md at 91ad0da44f64d41642d55e220d239648eef06196 · kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension · GitHub — Last surviving version of site-src/guides/standalone.md (commit 91ad0da4, the parent of deletion-commit ebbb148c dated 2026-06-30) -- the file 'Deploy As A Standalone Request Scheduler' guide, fetched as a primary-source substitute since the live docs page now returns empty content. States the EPP is 'a smart request scheduler for LLM requests' implementing prefix-cache-aware and load-aware scheduling; normally it runs as an ext-proc service fronting a proxy (GKE L7LB, Istio, agentgateway) for k8s-cluster model servers, which suits multi-tenant MaaS but is 'in practice an operational overhead' for single-tenant workloads like an offline RL post-training job sampler. Standalone mode solves this by deploying a proxy (Envoy or agentgateway) as a sidecar to the EPP communicating over localhost, via a Helm chart (oci://us-central1-docker.pkg.dev/k8s-staging-images/gateway-api-inference-extension/charts/standalone). Two configuration sub-modes, both still built around k8s: 'With Inference APIs Support' -- EPP configured via the InferencePool CRD, full InferenceObjective/priority support; and 'Without Inference APIs Support' -- 'EPP is configured using command line flags. This is the simplest method for standalone jobs which does not require installing the inference extension apis... no support for the features expressed using the inference APIs.' Even flag-only mode still targets k8s Pods via the Helm value inferenceExtension.endpointsServer.endpointSelector=app=vllm-qwen3-32b (a pod label selector) with inferenceExtension.endpointsServer.createInferencePool=false; there is no non-k8s endpoint source documented for standalone mode. Explicitly states Agentgateway standalone does not support InferencePool. The whole guide, including this flag-only path, was deleted from the docs site in the 2026-06-30 cleanup with no direct replacement page found. `evergreen` (1139 words)
- [[gomod]] — go.mod — go.mod for github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router at HEAD (main): module floor is 'go 1.26.6'; direct requires include sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime v0.24.1, k8s.io/{api,apimachinery,client-go,apiextensions-apiserver,component-base} v0.36.4, sigs.k8s.io/gateway-api v1.5.1, sigs.k8s.io/gateway-api-inference-extension v1.5.0 -- confirming controller-runtime and the full core k8s.io client stack are unconditional module-level dependencies of the whole repo (imported by cmd/epp/main.go and cmd/epp/runner/runner.go even when EndpointDiscovery is configured to skip the k8s API server at runtime, per docs/discovery.md). No 'replace' directives, no vendored forks, nothing pinning the module to a private/internal fork. This is a STRUCTURAL non-blocker for out-of-tree import (a downstream go.mod just needs go>=1.26.6 and must tolerate pulling in controller-runtime + k8s.io/client-go as build dependencies even in non-k8s deployments) but IS a real, if modest, cost: the binary size and dependency surface of a 'thin downstream main()' stays k8s-shaped regardless of target backend. `evergreen` (484 words)

- [[kv-cache-routing-why-standard-load-balancers-break-prefix-caching-and-how-to-fix]] — kv-cache-routing-why-standard-load-balancers-break-prefix-caching-and-how-to-fix-it — TrueFoundry vendor blog (June 2026) explains why standard round-robin/session-affinity load balancers destroy prefix-cache locality in multi-replica vLLM/SGLang deployments, and cites production llm-d benchmark numbers as evidence: on 8 pods / 16 H100 GPUs, llm-d's prefix-cache-aware routing measured 57x faster time-to-first-token and 2x throughput versus round-robin on identical hardware; a separate Llama-3.1-70B benchmark on 4x AMD MI300X GPUs showed 3x output tokens/sec and 2x TTFT reduction after switching to cache-aware routing; DigitalOcean's inference gateway (built on the same primitives) measured up to 108% throughput improvement for cache-aware vs. random routing. The post frames three escalating levels of prefix-aware routing and names their structural failure modes: Level 1 (session/sticky affinity by user ID) breaks on scale-out because adding a replica reshuffles the hash ring, triggering cache misses until the fleet re-warms; Level 2 (prefix-hash routing on the stable prompt prefix) requires correctly choosing the prefix boundary; Level 3 (KV-event-aware routing) subscribes to KV block allocation/eviction events from each inference engine, scores replicas on cache-overlap fraction weighted against current load, and is explicitly identified as the architecture used by llm-d's Endpoint Picker component. The piece explicitly frames cache locality and load balancing as pulling in opposite directions and states prefix-aware routing should be skipped when prompts are unique, short (<~200 tokens), or workloads lack structural repetition. `evergreen` (2726 words)
- [[kvblock-package-githubcomllm-dllm-d-routerpkgkvcachekvblock-go-packages]] — kvblock package - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/kvcache/kvblock - Go Packages — GoDoc reference for github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/kvcache/kvblock (v0.10.0) -- the KV-Block Index package implementing block-key hashing and the dual-key store. Key types: BlockHash is a uint64 (fits an FNV-64a output); TokenProcessor (backed by ChunkedTokenDatabase, created via NewChunkedTokenDatabase) 'chunks tokens into fixed-size blocks and hashes each block, chaining the previous block's hash so a key encodes its full prefix' -- default BlockSizeTokens is 16 (0 resolves to default). ComputeBlockExtraFeatures 'matches vLLM's _gen_mm_extra_hash_keys() algorithm' and 'matches vLLM v0.18.0+ which appends mm_feature.identifier per overlapping item' (MMHash mirrors vLLM's per-block extra_keys format); ParseRawExtraKeys also accepts the legacy [string,int] tuple format for pre-0.18 vLLM.\n\nCRITICAL HASHSEED DETAIL (tension with the llm-d-kv-cache architecture.md doc): TokenProcessorConfig.HashSeed's godoc comment reads verbatim: 'HashSeed is used to prefix initial hash chunks, similarly to vLLM's NONE_HASH. This should be aligned with vLLM's PYTHONHASHSEED environment variable. The system's deployer is responsible for aligning the vLLM deployments with the same seed value.' This directly contradicts (or at least is in tension with) the architecture.md doc's claim that the indexer's request-key space 'does not require alignment with any engine-side hash seed' -- the godoc explicitly places the alignment burden on the deployer. Neither source states the underlying hash algorithm name (SHA-256/xxhash/FNV) inside this package doc itself (that detail -- FNV-64a over CBOR -- comes from architecture.md, not from this godoc page).\n\nDual-key index API: Index interface exposes Add(engineKeys, requestKeys, entries), Lookup(requestKeys, podIdentifierSet), Evict(key, keyType, entries) where keyType is EngineKey or RequestKey, and GetRequestKey(engineKey) -> requestKey. Three Index implementations ship: InMemoryIndex, CostAwareMemoryIndex, and RedisIndex (Redis backend pages the keyspace with SCAN, stores JSON-encoded PodEntry per hash field, skips 'engine:' prefixed keys). KeyType.RequestKey variant is 'used for speculative entries that were added without engineKey mapping.' No mention of SGLang or TensorRT-LLM anywhere in this package's godoc. `evergreen` (3894 words)
- [[kvblock-inmemoryindexlookup-tests-emptiness-and-reads-entries-without-the-pod-ca]] — kvblock: InMemoryIndex.Lookup tests emptiness and reads entries without the pod-cache lock · Issue #2698 · llm-d/llm-d-router — GitHub issue #2698 (opened Sep 4, 2026, llm-d/llm-d-router, by collaborator nilig) is a precise concurrency-bug writeup in kvblock.InMemoryIndex.Lookup: the method checks whether a key's pod-cache is empty and then reads its holder keys as two SEPARATELY locked calls (not atomic), even though PodCache.mu exists for exactly this purpose and writers (Add, Evict) correctly hold it across their check-then-act sequences. If a concurrent Evict or Clear removes the last entry for a key between Lookup's two calls, Keys() returns empty, causing (1) the unfiltered branch to assign an empty slice into the result map -- producing a key that is 'present but held by nobody,' contradicting the early-stop guard meant to prevent exactly this -- and (2) the prefix-chain-break check being skipped in both branches, letting the returned map carry keys from beyond where the chain should have stopped. States the bug does NOT corrupt routing today because the downstream matcher walks keys in order and stops at the first empty-entries key regardless, so the cost is only wasted lookups/materialization, not wrong routing decisions. The visible symptom was in telemetry: keysFound counted map entries rather than entries-with-holders, so an empty key was published as a false block-hit; PR #2669 already fixed the metric by counting only keys with >=1 entry. The full fix, PR #2680, rewrites Lookup to read entries and count in a single critical section under PodCache.mu via a new PodCache.filteredEntries method, storing a result only when the filtered slice is non-empty. Notes this is not a Go data race (go -race will never catch it) since each locked call is individually safe -- only the sequence across calls is wrong -- and the race window is only a few instructions wide, though evictions run continuously from BlockRemoved events, pod clears, and LRU capacity pressure. `evergreen` (708 words)
- [[kvbmmd]] — kvbm.md — NVIDIA Dynamo KV Block Manager docs: Dynamo's own cache tier spanning GPU, host memory, SSD and object storage, with LMCache and FlexKV as parallel optional backends — one of three structurally independent distributed KV-cache implementations that shipped within roughly twelve months. `review` (759 words)
- [[kvcache-package-githubcomllm-dllm-d-routerpkgkvcache-go-packages]] — kvcache package - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/kvcache - Go Packages — Go package doc for llm-d-router's pkg/kvcache (v0.10.0), the read side of the KV-cache subsystem. The Indexer (constructed via NewKVCacheIndexer) scores model-serving pods by KV-cache locality: ComputeBlockKeysFromTokens runs the injected kvblock.TokenProcessor to chunk pre-tokenized input into fixed-size blocks and hashes each block while chaining in the previous block's hash so a key encodes its whole prefix (extraFeatures taints the hash with per-block multimodal metadata). ScoreTokens then queries the kvblock.Index for which pods hold each block key (optionally restricted to a caller-supplied pod set) and reduces the lookup to per-pod scores via a pluggable KVBlockScorer. The default LongestPrefixScorer credits each pod for its longest run of consecutive block hits starting from block 0, weighted per device tier via KVCacheBackendConfig (Name/Weight, e.g. gpu/cpu/disk media). Tokenization is explicitly external -- TokenizersPoolConfig/internal tokenization is marked Deprecated in favor of calling ScoreTokens with tokens already computed. Index and scorer are wrapped with OpenTelemetry tracing (no-op when unconfigured). This package, along with kvblock and kvevents, was internalized from llm-d-kv-cache into llm-d-router per PR #1886. `evergreen` (1247 words)
- [[kvcacheutilization-package-githubcomllm-dllm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkpluginssched]] — kvcacheutilization package - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/plugins/scheduling/scorer/kvcacheutilization - Go Packages — GoDoc reference (llm-d-router v0.10.0) for the kv-cache-utilization-scorer plugin. Exact formula: score(endpoint) = 1 - kvCacheUsagePercent, read from endpoint metrics via metrics.KVCacheUsagePercentKey, so lower KV-cache usage yields a higher score (Distribution category -- spreads traffic away from high-KV-pressure endpoints). No runtime parameters; wired into a scheduling profile with a weight. Also documents multicluster-kv-cache-utilization-scorer, a cluster-scoped variant (added v0.10.0) that scores on a pool-level llm-d.ai/multicluster-kv-cache-utilization attribute rather than a per-pod scrape -- requires configuring a multicluster-metrics-data-source (scrapes the peer cluster) and multicluster-metrics-extractor in dataLayer, or the scorer silently returns no score for that endpoint. `evergreen` (744 words)
- [[kvevents-package-githubcomllm-dllm-d-routerpkgkvevents-go-packages]] — kvevents package - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/kvevents - Go Packages — Go package doc for llm-d-router's pkg/kvevents (v0.10.0), the write side of the KV-cache subsystem that keeps kvblock.Index current. vLLM and SGLang engines publish block-stored / block-removed / all-cleared events over ZMQ as they admit/evict KV blocks; a SubscriberManager maintains one ZMQ subscription per engine endpoint (created/torn down as pods churn via EnsureSubscriber/RemoveSubscriber), and each message is decoded by a per-engine EngineAdapter (ParseMessage extracts pod ID, model name, and an EventBatch; ShardingKey ensures same-source messages process in order). A sharded worker Pool (NewPool) fans decoded events out to workers keyed by sharding key -- guaranteeing ordering per source -- and applies Add/Evict calls to the kvblock.Index; NewPool also registers kvcache metrics with the controller-runtime registry (idempotent via sync.Once). A reference-count dedup filter ensures a block removal is forwarded to the index only once no remaining announcement references the block, preventing premature eviction when multiple engines/HMA groups share a block; suppressed vs forwarded removals are counted in metrics. Event types are typed structs: BlockStoredEvent (BlockHashes, Tokens, ParentHash, BlockSize, DeviceTier, LoraID/LoraName, GroupIdx, KVCacheSpecKind e.g. full_attention/mla_attention/sliding_window/mamba/chunked_local_attention/sink_full_attention/encoder_only_attention/cross_attention, KVCacheSpecSlidingWindowSize), BlockRemovedEvent (BlockHashes, DeviceTier, GroupIdx), and AllBlocksClearedEvent (DeviceTier). Config exposes ZMQEndpoint, TopicFilter (e.g. 'kv@'), Concurrency, EngineType ('vllm' or 'sglang', default vllm), and optional Kubernetes-based DiscoverPods/PodDiscoveryConfig (PodLabelSelector, PodNamespace, SocketPort default 5557, ReplaySocketPort for replay requests, disabled when unset). `evergreen` (1908 words)
- [[llamacpp-vs-ollama-running-llms-locally-picovoice]] — llama.cpp vs. ollama: Running LLMs Locally - Picovoice — Picovoice vendor blog post, originally published May 13, 2024, updated July 23, 2024 -- STALE relative to this run's 18-24 month recency window and explicitly superseded on one load-bearing technical claim: this piece states 'ollama... built on llama.cpp' and frames Ollama purely as a llama.cpp wrapper, but the HN thread in this same batch (named Ollama maintainer mchiang, 2026) states Ollama has since moved off llama.cpp as a wrapper for its new multimodal engine, keeping only the shared GGML library and implementing its own model code and MXFP4 kernels -- so this source's core framing is out of date. Retained for background only: llama.cpp was created by Georgi Gerganov in March 2023; Ollama was started by Jeffrey Morgan in July 2023; both are MIT-licensed. Enterprise-adoption caveats raised (generic, not concurrency-specific): no official support/SLA, reliance on community support, documentation 'less comprehensive... especially for those who do not have machine learning expertise,' unpredictable maintenance/release cadence (llama.cpp cited as having 'close to 2,000 releases'), and a generic open-source supply-chain security warning citing the 2024 xz/liblzma backdoor as a cautionary analogy (not a vulnerability in llama.cpp/ollama themselves). `review` (1124 words)
- [[llm-d]] — llm-d — Official landing page for llm-d (llm-d.ai), an open-source, Kubernetes-native stack for distributed LLM inference, CNCF Sandbox project (accepted Mar 12 2026, donated by IBM Research, Red Hat, and Google Cloud at KubeCon Europe 2026). - [[llm-d-distributed-inference-serving-on-kubernetes-kgateway]] — llm-d - Distributed Inference Serving on Kubernetes – kgateway — kgateway.dev blog post (Christian Posta, Solo.io, May 20 2025) covering llm-d's launch announcement (Red Hat/Google/IBM). Frames llm-d's two pillars as (1) disaggregation of prefill/decode phases across specialized workers and (2) an intelligent routing layer built on the Gateway API Inference Extension (GIE) and kgateway (Envoy-based), with Istio as an alternative data-plane option. Describes kgateway's Endpoint Selection Extension implementing the Endpoint Picker Protocol: examines model name, LoRA adapter requirements, and request criticality, then selects backend by queue depth, KV-cache utilization, and adapter availability, enabling prioritization of critical requests and opportunistic dropping of sheddable ones. Cites the llm-d announcement's benchmark claims of up to 3x improvement in time-to-first-token and roughly 2x throughput under SLO constraints versus standard load balancing. Confirms llm-d 'introduces its own scheduler that implements the EPP' on top of the InferenceModel/InferencePool CRDs -- i.e., the EPP/GIE contract (not kgateway or Istio specifically) is the reusable abstraction boundary a non-k8s backend implementation would need to satisfy. `evergreen` (771 words)
- [[llm-d-04-achieve-sota-performance-across-accelerators-llm-d]] — llm-d 0.4: Achieve SOTA Performance Across Accelerators | llm-d — llm-d v0.4 release blog focused on reducing end-to-end request latency (complementing v0.3's throughput-oriented wide expert parallelism). Reports over 40% per-token latency reduction for DeepSeek V3.1 on H200 via speculative decoding (including DeepSeek's native MTP support, plus a new speculators OSS project), async scheduling, and block-FP8 kernel fusion. Adds dynamic P/D disaggregation to Google TPU (over datacenter TCP) and Intel XPU, plus AWS EFA network support. Formalizes a new well-lit path for tiered prefix-cache offloading, combining vLLM-native CPU offloading and LMCache connectors so KV blocks swap to host CPU RAM instead of being dropped when VRAM fills; benchmark shows CPU offloading (100GB) cuts mean TTFT 25.6% (9.0s to 6.7s) and raises throughput 21.3% (38,535 to 46,751 tok/s) versus baseline vLLM when the KV working set exceeds HBM capacity — LMCache's CPU offloading connector performs comparably (-27.8% TTFT, +21.7% throughput). Enhances intelligent scheduling with tier-aware prefix-cache affinity (configurable weighted scoring, e.g. weighting GPU hits above CPU hits) and introduces the no-hit-lru-scorer, which distributes 'cold' requests (new prefix-cache seeds) to prevent hotspot formation; on 8x Qwen3-32B/16x H100 at 50 QPS with 150 shared prefixes, this cut P99 TTFT in half (18.3s to 8s) by eliminating wait-queue oscillation seen in v0.3. Introduces the experimental Workload Variant Autoscaler (WVA) using reactive saturation-based scaling (queue length + KV-cache utilization signals, one-replica-at-a-time increments). Notes a breaking Helm chart change: Inference Scheduler config flags move from array to map format. `evergreen` (1905 words)
- [[llm-d-05-sustaining-performance-at-scale-llm-d]] — llm-d 0.5: Sustaining Performance at Scale | llm-d — llm-d v0.5 release blog ('Sustaining Performance at Scale'), shifting focus from peak latency (v0.4) to operational rigor: reproducible benchmarking, Hierarchical KV Offloading, LoRA/HA-aware scheduling, resilient networking, and scale-to-zero autoscaling. Introduces the llm-d FS backend (a storage connector on vLLM's native offloading interface) establishing a 3-tier memory hierarchy (GPU/CPU/Disk): decouples cache capacity from local node memory, enables cross-replica reuse via a shared persistent KV store (new nodes 'hydrate' from the shared tier instead of cold-starting), and uses async I/O with parallel workers. Benchmark on Llama-3.1-70B / 4x H100 with IBM Storage Scale shows GPU-only deployments collapse once HBM saturates, while storage-backed offload sustains ~185k tok/s at 250 concurrent users (13.9x improvement over collapse). Scheduling advances: active-active HA via a KV-Events subscription manager with dynamic pod discovery (ZMQ subscriptions tied to pod lifecycle) and LoRA-precise prefix caching that routes by adapter-specific cache locality to avoid the 'thundering herd' problem. Networking: contributes a UCCL (Unified Collective Communication Library) transport backend to NIXL for P/D KV transfer — under network congestion, UCCL degraded only 7.1% (359ms to 384ms) vs UCX's 17.1% (362ms to 424ms) on a 4GB KV-cache payload over 200Gb/s OpenShift interconnects, a 2.4x resilience improvement. Also reports Wide-EP throughput on B200 (~50k output tok/s at 16P/16D) and inference-scheduling latency gains on Qwen3-32B/H100 (up to 109% higher throughput, 99% lower TTFT vs baseline k8s Service). Roadmap: predictive TTFT/TPOT-based routing, proactive KV 'pinning' APIs, and end-to-end distributed tracing. `evergreen` (2180 words)
- [[llm-d-router-llm-d]] — llm-d Router | llm-d — llm-d docs page defining the llm-d Router as Proxy + Endpoint Picker (EPP). Proxy is any conformant L7 proxy (Istio, agentgateway, Envoy AI Gateway, cloud ALBs) handling data-plane concerns; EPP holds the routing intelligence. Clarifies terminology: 'llm-d Router' = Proxy+EPP; 'Inference Gateway' = llm-d Router in Gateway Mode; 'Request Scheduler' = the EPP sub-component doing filter/score. Confirms the EPP evaluates requests against InferencePool state (KV-cache locality, load, priority) and returns a model-server pod address to the Proxy. `evergreen` (466 words)
- [[llm-d-flow-control-priority-queuing-for-shared-gpu-inference-red-hat-developer]] — llm-d flow control: Priority queuing for shared GPU inference | Red Hat Developer — Red Hat Developer article (Aug 27 2026) explaining llm-d's Flow Control layer via the v0.10.0 release tag, giving a durable Go source path: pkg/epp/framework/plugins/flowcontrol/saturationdetector/utilization/detector.go (lines 119-164) for the utilization detector that fuses per-replica queue depth and KV-cache pressure into one pool-saturation signal. Clarifies the two-stage decision split inside the EPP: Flow Control (admission -- 'when') evaluates priority bands high-to-low, applies a per-band fairness policy to pick a tenant queue then an ordering policy to pick the next request, entirely BEFORE backend Scheduling (placement -- 'where') runs; the scheduler only then selects the highest-scored available vLLM pod for Envoy to forward to. Priority comes from an InferenceObjective CRD mapping a workload to an integer (higher = higher priority); requests at equal priority share a band and are arbitrated by the tenant fairness policy. A request held in the Endpoint Picker's central queue is released on capacity, TTL expiry, or a configured queue-limit rejection. This is the source that pins down the actual Go package path for the saturation-detector plugin interface, useful for chasing the extension-point contract for anyone customizing scoring/admission for non-pod backends. `evergreen` (2699 words)
- [[llm-d-in-action-epp-prefix-cache-routing-and-what-it-actually-means-karthika-rag]] — llm-d in Action — EPP Prefix Cache Routing and What It Actually Means - Karthika Raghavan — Predecessor hands-on post (Karthika Raghavan, April 2026) to the same author's PD-disaggregation failure post, documenting llm-d v0.4.0's aggregated-serving EPP prefix-cache routing on a single Lambda Labs GH200 480GB GPU (Qwen3-0.6B, Locust multi-tenant load test). This is the baseline the later PD-disaggregation post's 6.5x/3.7x/3.5x degradation numbers are measured against: E2E latency p50=150ms/p95=260ms/p99=300ms (flat, no queue buildup), ITL flat at 5ms (200 tok/s/request, no eviction pressure), aggregate prefix cache hit rate 81.1% with per-instance hit rate steady at ~80% throughout the test (a sustained plateau, not a decaying burst), and KV Cache Hit Rate described elsewhere in the post as 80.6% overall (roughly 4-in-5 requests reuse cached KV, 19.4% are cold full-prefill). The author explicitly flags a scaling limit relevant to the adversarial routing question: this 81% hit rate was achieved with only three tenant profiles (~200-token system prompts) on a SINGLE pod; as tenant count scales to 50, 500, or 5000, the working set of distinct system prompts grows beyond what one pod's KV cache can hold, cache hit rate degrades, and TTFT rises -- not because EPP routing is broken, but because it becomes a KV-cache-capacity problem requiring either larger per-pod KV budgets, more pods with affinity-based routing (which reintroduces the hotspot/load-imbalance tradeoff), or tiered KV offloading to host memory (enabled architecturally by GH200's 900 GB/s NVLink-C2C CPU-GPU unified memory, 7x PCIe Gen5 bandwidth, though not exercised in this experiment). Also states a general operating threshold: once cache hit rate drops below ~50%, no amount of tuning recovers tail latency, and the correct intervention is upstream (better routing, larger KV budgets, or PD separation) rather than local tuning. `evergreen` (4723 words)
- [[llm-d-on-kubernetes-disaggregated-llm-inference-deployment-guide-2026-spheron-bl]] — llm-d on Kubernetes: Disaggregated LLM Inference Deployment Guide (2026) | Spheron Blog — Vendor blog (Spheron, low authority, but useful for architecture framing and a competitor contrast) covering llm-d's March 24, 2026 acceptance into CNCF Sandbox (backed by IBM, Red Hat, Google, CoreWeave, NVIDIA). Names four core components: the llm-d scheduler (cache-aware routing process tracking per-decode-worker KV cache state), the InferencePool CRD (groups decode GPU pods into a named pool the scheduler targets), the InferenceObjective CRD (maps model/LoRA-adapter names to backend deployments with traffic weights and criticality labels), and Gateway API Inference Extension integration. Explicitly contrasts llm-d against NVIDIA Dynamo: Dynamo is an orchestration layer that runs ABOVE vLLM and OUTSIDE Kubernetes, using NIXL for KV transfer and coupled to NVIDIA DGX/HGX hardware, whereas llm-d uses standard Kubernetes networking, CRDs, and Gateway API -- a relevant data point for anyone considering an out-of-cluster router design, since Dynamo demonstrates that non-k8s-native orchestration for the same KV-aware routing problem is an established pattern outside llm-d itself. Notes LMCache as an option for sharing KV state across llm-d workers via a Redis-backed tier. `evergreen` (3884 words)
- [[llm-d-v07-from-feature-introduction-to-production-hardening-llm-d]] — llm-d v0.7: From Feature Introduction to Production Hardening | llm-d — llm-d v0.7 release blog ('From Feature Introduction to Production Hardening') -- highly load-bearing for router/non-k8s research.

RENAME: v0.7 renames the core component to the 'llm-d Router' -- 'the functional combination of a Proxy and an Endpoint Picker (EPP)' -- replacing the prior 'inference scheduler'/'inference gateway' terminology, explicitly because those terms 'conflicted with Kubernetes scheduling concepts and was too narrowly tied to the Gateway API.' The repository was renamed to github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router. This is the component the user's query wants to customize.

STANDALONE MODE: v0.7 makes 'Standalone Mode' the DEFAULT deployment: runs with a generic proxy (Envoy) rather than requiring the full Gateway API Inference Extension (GAIE) stack -- 'go from clone to serving in minutes, and layer on gateway features as your deployment matures.' Full GAIE remains available (bumped to v1.5.0, Istio 1.29.2, AgentGateway v1.1.0) but is no longer the default entry point. This is a direct architectural loosening of the router's coupling to the full Kubernetes Gateway API stack, though standalone mode still targets Kubernetes-deployed pods, not non-k8s backends.

KUSTOMIZE MIGRATION: entire installation surface moved from Helm/Helmfile to Kustomize; llm-d-infra and llm-d-modelservice Helm charts deprecated; monolithic Helmfile CI replaced by ci-kustomize-dry-run.yaml. Every guide (optimized baseline, prefix-cache routing, P/D disaggregation, workload-variant autoscaling, Intel XPU) now Kustomize-based with per-platform overlays (GKE, OpenShift, etc.) -- still Kubernetes-native tooling.

DOCUMENTATION: >10,000 new lines; new Architecture Reference covers EPP design (scheduling, flow control, request handling), KV management (KV Indexer, KV Offloader, routing-plugin integration), disaggregation ops (KV cache transfer, NIXL, routing sidecar coordination), autoscaling, latency predictor; new API reference documents EPP HTTP headers, EndpointPickerConfig, InferenceModelRewrite, InferenceObjective, InferencePool CRDs.

HARDWARE/P-D: CUDA 13.0.2 (from 12.9.1, requires NVIDIA driver 580+) with Blackwell/GB200 support (llm-d-cuda-gb200 image), vLLM bumped to 0.19.1 (neuralmagic fork), LMCache v0.4.4, FlashInfer v0.6.6; added Rebellions vendor, AMD ROCm guide, Intel XPU TP=2 for Qwen3-32B, TPU v6e/v7. P/D disaggregation extended to Google TPU v7 (Qwen 3.5 guide) and Oracle Cloud Infrastructure, plus a new three-part disaggregation architecture series covering KV cache transfer, NIXL, and routing-sidecar coordination.

NEW EXPERIMENTAL FEATURES: Flow Control -- centralized request queuing/admission control AT THE ROUTER LEVEL, classifying requests by a FlowKey (Fairness ID + Priority); EPP keeps per-flow in-memory queues, dispatches by priority/fairness/ordering; integrates with InferenceObjective CRDs for SLO enforcement. Batch Gateway -- OpenAI-compatible /v1/batches and /v1/files API for jobs up to 50,000 requests, with API Server / Batch Processor / Garbage Collector components; pluggable storage (PostgreSQL or Redis/Valkey for metadata, Redis/Valkey for queues, S3 or filesystem for files).

KV CACHE: AWS EFS backend added for tiered prefix-cache offload (GPU HBM -> CPU DRAM -> NVMe/EFS) integrating with vLLM's OffloadingConnector; real-time prefix-cache-aware routing now default (replacing hashSeed workarounds); predicted-latency-based scheduling rewritten.

CI: rewrote CI from monolithic per-accelerator scripts to per-guide nightly matrices across CKS/GKE/OpenShift; net -2,785 lines of YAML across 63 files despite more coverage. v0.7 saw 3.5x the PR volume of v0.6 and 23 new contributors (Rebellions, Intel, AMD, others).

*** MOST DIRECTLY RELEVANT TO THE RESEARCH QUERY ***: The 'What Is Next' (v0.8 roadmap) section states under 'Reinforcement learning infrastructure': 'Non-Kubernetes deployment mode enables llm-d in Slurm clusters and research labs through file-based service discovery.' This is an explicit, named llm-d roadmap item (as of the v0.7 blog, targeting v0.8, tracked at github.com/llm-d/llm-d/issues/1520) for running llm-d OUTSIDE Kubernetes using file-based (not InferencePool-based) service discovery -- directly on point for customizing the router to support non-k8s backends. Also on the v0.8 roadmap: NIXL gets TTL-based cache eviction and request draining for graceful pod termination; KV transfer connectors expand beyond NVIDIA to Mooncake (Alibaba) and MoRI (Microsoft). `evergreen` (2577 words)
- [[llm-d-v08-from-platform-to-control-plane-llm-d]] — llm-d v0.8: From Platform to Control Plane | llm-d — llm-d.ai v0.8 release blog ('From Platform to Control Plane', targeted ~mid-2026, v0.9 milestone referenced as 'early August'). This is the release that FIRST shipped the FileDiscovery plugin under the heading 'Beyond Kubernetes: RL, Slurm, and File Discovery' -- states it 'enables llm-d's routing layer to discover inference endpoints through filesystem-based service discovery rather than the Kubernetes API,' unlocking Slurm clusters and bare-metal research labs, motivated primarily by RL rollout workloads that run on Slurm rather than Kubernetes. Also documents component renames relevant to any fork/customization plan: llm-d-inference-scheduler -> llm-d-router-endpoint-picker, and llm-d-routing-sidecar -> llm-d-router-disagg-sidecar (both now explicitly named as components of the llm-d Router, not standalone systems). Confirms llm-d dropped custom vLLM image builds in favor of upstream vllm/vllm-openai:v0.23.0 (control-plane-not-fork positioning) and made SGLang a first-class second backend engine alongside vLLM across most well-lit paths (optimized-baseline, precise-prefix-cache-routing, tiered-prefix-cache, P/D disaggregation), plus added TensorRT-LLM as a third engine via trtllm-serve. Notes GAIE (Gateway API Inference Extension) dependency pinned to v1.5.0 at this release, with GAIE Helm charts published from the llm-d-router OCI registry. Workload Variant Autoscaler (WVA) CRD migrated to the llm-d.ai API group (breaking change). `evergreen` (2286 words)
- [[llm-d-v09-hardened-for-scale-llm-d]] — llm-d v0.9: Hardened for Scale | llm-d — llm-d.ai v0.9 release blog ('Hardened for Scale', v0.10 milestone targets September 2026). Introduces a formal Plugin Stability Lifecycle classifying every router plugin as Alpha/Beta/Stable; Alpha plugins require the --allow-experimental-plugins flag to activate, and automated dependency validation checks a plugin's prerequisites before router startup (this lifecycle gate is directly relevant to any custom non-k8s discovery/backend plugin, which would likely start Alpha). Adds Router HA: a multi-EPP topology where non-leader replicas keep their datastores populated and continue serving (no 503 on standby), plus a --drain-timeout flag for graceful shutdown. Flow control ships production-safe defaults (defaultRequestTTL=60s, per-band maxRequests=5000) and distinguishes empty-pool 503 from retryable-backpressure 429. Adds GPU-utilization-aware routing via a DCGM extractor plus a 'generic endpoint-attribute filter and scorer' (a generalized, non-engine-specific routing primitive potentially reusable for custom backend metrics). Confirms RL integration work is incubation-stage in llm-d-incubation/llm-d-rl and llm-d-rl-time-slicing, with framework integrations (veRL, vime) replacing each framework's built-in load balancer with llm-d's EPP scoring/filtering; links a time-slicing architecture proposal at llm-d/llm-d/proposals/rl-time-slicing-platform.md. Names 'a standardized model server interface' as a v0.10 roadmap item: 'a common API to query engine capabilities, manage lifecycle, and configure runtime parameters - independent of whether the engine underneath is vLLM, SGLang, or TensorRT-LLM' -- directly relevant to designing a custom non-pod backend abstraction. Notes EPP KV metrics moved to the llm_d_router_epp subsystem (deprecated aliases available during transition) and default vLLM image updated to v0.26.0. `evergreen` (2217 words)
- [[llm-d-kv-cache-manager-module-githubcomllm-dllm-d-kv-cache-manager-go-packages]] — llm-d-kv-cache-manager module - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache-manager - Go Packages — Go Packages page for github.com/llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache-manager (v0.4.0), the pluggable KV-Cache Indexer library. Confirms architecture: kvcache.Indexer orchestrates; kvblock.Index is the block-hash-to-pod map; kvevents.Pool is the KVEvents ingestion component (write path, ingests events emitted by vLLM pods). Read path: scheduler calls Indexer.Score(prompt, pods) -> Indexer queries kvblock.Index -> returns per-pod cache-hit scores. Write path: vLLM pods emit KVEvents on block create/evict -> Event Subscriber (kvevents.Pool) updates kvblock.Index in near-real-time. Directly relevant to non-Kubernetes backends: ships a **Valkey Backend** example ('examples/valkey_example/README.md') showing how to configure Valkey (Redis-compatible) as the storage backend for KV-block indexing, including RDMA support — i.e., the block index itself can be backed by an external key-value store rather than only in-process memory, which is a plausible integration point for a router that needs to track non-k8s-pod endpoints. Also ships a kv_events/offline example using 'a dummy ZMQ publisher' (no live vLLM/pod required) and a kv_events/online example against a real vLLM Helm deployment — the offline example is evidence that the ZMQ event-consumer side can be exercised against any process that speaks the same ZMQ pub/sub + msgpack wire format, independent of Kubernetes. Points to docs/architecture.md and docs/configuration.md at the v0.4.0 tag for deeper detail (not yet fetched). `evergreen` (602 words)
- [[llm-d-kv-cachedocsarchitecturemd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-kv-cache-github]] — llm-d-kv-cache/docs/architecture.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache · GitHub — GROUND-TRUTH architecture doc (github.com/llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache, docs/architecture.md, commit 1d8e62e, May 10 2026; code-owners include vMaroon of IBM Research) for the KV-Cache Indexer Go library -- this is the single most load-bearing source for the router-customization question. Confirms this repo is the successor/rename of llm-d-kv-cache-manager (the fetch of the old repo's v0.4.0 valkey_example URL redirected here). Key findings directly on the focus questions:

(1) ENDPOINT IDENTITY: ZMQ topic format is literally "kv@<pod-ip>:<port>@<model>" -- pod identity in the index is a network address (IP:port), not a Kubernetes pod name/UID. The VLLMAdapter "parses kv@<pod-ip>:<port>@<model-name> topic strings." PodEntry objects in the index carry this IP:port identity plus DeviceTier and a Speculative flag.

(2) PUSH VS PULL -- the doc explicitly describes TWO SUPPORTED "Event Delivery Modes," which is the key architectural fact for non-Kubernetes backends: "Centralized -- every model-server pod connects (zmq.PUB) to a single endpoint hosted by the host process (zmq.SUB). Simpler to configure; works naturally with a single host replica" (pods dial out to a fixed, known host address -- no pod discovery needed at all, host just binds tcp://*:5557 and listens). Versus "Pod discovery -- each model-server pod binds its own ZMQ socket; the host process discovers pods via Kubernetes label selectors and creates per-pod subscribers. This mode supports active-active multi-replica deployments -- each replica independently subscribes to every pod and sees the full event stream." So: in Centralized mode the connection is pod-initiated (push, no discovery mechanism required, backend-agnostic); in Pod-discovery mode the connection is host/indexer-initiated per pod (host acts as ZMQ client dialing out), and pod discovery is explicitly Kubernetes-label-selector-based in the shipped implementation.

(3) DISCOVERY MECHANISM: confirmed Kubernetes-specific in Pod-discovery mode ("the host process discovers pods via Kubernetes label selectors") -- this is the piece that would need to be swapped out for a non-Kubernetes backend. The Centralized delivery mode is the natural escape hatch: it requires zero discovery infrastructure since pods proactively connect to a static, configured host endpoint.

(4) IDENTITY MAPPING / DUAL-KEY DESIGN: the indexer cannot derive a "request key" (its own FNV-64a-over-CBOR hash of token chunks, used for scoring lookups from a prompt) from an "engine key" (the model server's own internal content-addressing hash for a block, carried in KV-Events) or vice versa -- the two hash spaces are independent. The bridge is built on ingestion: BlockStored events carry both the engine key and the token chunk, so the worker computes the request key from tokens and records an engine_key -> request_key mapping; BlockRemoved events carry only the engine key and use that mapping to find and evict the request key. Scoring never touches engine keys. This dual-key bridge is orthogonal to pod identity (which is IP:port from the topic) -- separately, whatever the EPP/scheduler uses as its own "endpoint name" for a candidate pod (e.g., a Kubernetes Endpoint/pod IP from the InferencePool) must match the same IP:port string the indexer extracted from the ZMQ topic for the score lookup to resolve to the correct routable endpoint; the doc does not describe an explicit secondary identity-reconciliation step beyond this literal IP:port string match.

BLOCK INDEX BACKENDS (pluggable, relevant to non-k8s persistence/architecture choices): In-Memory two-level LRU (default, ~100M keys x 10 pod entries capacity), Cost-Aware Memory (Ristretto, byte-budgeted, for variable-size multimodal/LoRA entries), and Redis/Valkey (external TCP server, Valkey is Redis-wire-compatible/BSD-licensed with experimental RDMA transport) -- explicitly noted as adding "a network hop per lookup" and tying "host availability to the external store," with the caveat that shared external state is "rarely necessary since each in-memory replica converges from the event stream" via Pod-discovery mode's independent per-replica subscription.

Other architecturally load-bearing details: CPU offloading emits a second BlockStored with the same engine key, a device tier, but no tokens (content unchanged) -- relies on a strict GPU-then-CPU event-ordering guarantee from the same pod. KV-block hashing uses chained FNV-64a over CBOR(parentHash, tokenChunk, extra), block size default 16 tokens, seeded by a configurable hashSeed + model name; the "extra" field folds in LoRA adapter name/ID or multimodal content hashes so different adapters/modalities produce independent key chains. Internal tokenization (tokenization.Pool) is explicitly DEPRECATED; new integrations must tokenize externally (host process or sidecar) and call Indexer.ScoreTokens directly with token IDs -- the indexer is "agnostic to the tokenizer source as long as the resulting token IDs match what the engines emit." The pure-Go ZeroMQ implementation used means the indexer does not require libzmq on the host. Speculative entries (TTL-cache-based, caller-configured) close the blind spot between a routing decision and the confirming BlockStored event. Work-in-progress items: hybrid-attention-aware scoring (full/sliding-window/state-space groups evict independently) and correct data-parallel-rank handling for shared-frontend DP deployments (tracked in llm-d-kv-cache issue #357). `evergreen` (3084 words)
- [[llm-d-kv-cachedocsconfigurationmd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-kv-cache-github]] — llm-d-kv-cache/docs/configuration.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache · GitHub — GROUND-TRUTH configuration reference (github.com/llm-d/llm-d-kv-cache, docs/configuration.md, commit a746476, May 7 2026) -- gives the exact JSON config schema that operationalizes the architecture doc's two event-delivery modes, and is the most directly actionable source found for the router-customization question.

KV-EVENT POOL CONFIG explicitly supports two modes: "1. Static Endpoint Mode: Connects to a single ZMQ endpoint" and "2. Auto-Discovery Mode (default): Automatically discovers and subscribes to per-pod ZMQ endpoints." Static Endpoint Mode config: {"zmqEndpoint": "tcp://indexer:5557", "topicFilter": ..., "concurrency": 8, "engineType": "vllm", "discoverPods": false} -- and critically: "The zmqEndpoint field specifies the local ZMQ socket address to bind to." This means in Static mode the INDEXER (not the pods) binds a socket, and backend pods/processes connect outward to it as ZMQ PUB clients -- confirming this static mode requires ZERO Kubernetes API dependency and is usable today with any backend (VM, bare-metal, non-k8s container) that can run a ZMQ PUB client publishing to a "kv@..."-prefixed topic (topicFilter default "kv@") matching the wire format vLLM/SGLang use.

Auto-Discovery Mode (the k8s-coupled path, default discoverPods: true) is driven by a PodDiscoveryConfig: {"podLabelSelector": "llm-d.ai/inference-serving=true", "podNamespace": "", "socketPort": 5557} implemented as "the Kubernetes pod reconciler for automatic per-pod ZMQ subscriber management. The reconciler watches Kubernetes pods and dynamically creates/removes ZMQ subscribers based on pod lifecycle." Pod eligibility requires ALL FOUR conditions: label-selector match, pod.Status.Phase == Running, pod.Status.PodIP != "", and PodReady == ConditionTrue -- when any goes false the subscriber is automatically torn down. Requires explicit RBAC (Role/ClusterRole with get/list/watch on the "pods" resource) depending on whether podNamespace is scoped or empty (cluster-wide).

engineType field ("vllm" or "sglang", default "vllm") selects the EngineAdapter, confirming engine-adapter selection (not endpoint discovery) is the extension point for supporting additional inference engines' wire formats.

Also documents: InMemoryIndexConfig (size default 100,000,000 keys, podCacheSize per-key pod-entry cap), CostAwareMemoryIndexConfig (byte-budgeted, e.g. "2GiB"), and RedisIndexConfig (shared by both Redis and Valkey backends: address, backendType "redis"|"valkey", enableRDMA) -- "only the first available one will be used" among configured backends. TokenProcessorConfig's hashSeed is explicitly independent of vLLM's PYTHONHASHSEED ("no alignment with vLLM's PYTHONHASHSEED is required") because engine keys and request keys are decoupled per the dual-key design. tokenizersPoolConfig confirmed deprecated -- leaving it null makes the indexer skip in-process tokenization pool creation entirely, requiring callers to supply token IDs directly via ScoreTokens.

STRATEGIC IMPLICATION for the router-customization question: the KV-cache-indexer layer already ships a Kubernetes-independent code path (Static Endpoint Mode) that requires no changes to support non-Kubernetes backends for KV-event ingestion -- the customization burden for a non-k8s-aware llm-d router falls almost entirely on (a) how the EPP/router's own endpoint-pool membership and health state (analogous to the InferencePool/EndpointSlice watch) is populated for non-k8s backends, and (b) ensuring that whatever identity string a non-k8s backend uses for its own endpoint bookkeeping matches the IP:port the backend publishes in its ZMQ topic -- not on modifying the KV-cache indexer itself. `evergreen` (2361 words)

No Kubernetes Lease, coordination.k8s.io, or leader-election mechanism is mentioned anywhere in this document -- it does not apply to the coordinator, only to the separate EPP component (see operations.md). Also documents the EPP-Profile header-based routing, per-phase scheduling profiles (encode/prefill/decode), the conditional-decode 412 cache-miss handshake, and pluggable KV/EC transfer connectors (kv-nixl, kv-sglang, kv-shared-storage; ec-nixl, ec-shared-storage) selected by config, none of which involve Kubernetes APIs. `evergreen` (5585 words)
