- [[llm-d-routerdocsdiscoverymd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-router-github]] — llm-d-router/docs/discovery.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE, HIGHEST VALUE FOR THE RESEARCH QUERY. Canonical docs/discovery.md on llm-d/llm-d-router main branch (fetched Sep 11 2026, unpinned commit; referenced as new documentation delivered by PR #1007). This is the definitive, most detailed spec of the discovery subsystem and RESOLVES a config-path discrepancy seen across other sources: the authoritative field path is dataLayer.discovery.endpoints.pluginRef (confirmed by two full worked YAML examples), NOT the blog's abbreviated dataLayer.discovery.pluginRef nor the discovery.go source comment's EndpointPickerConfig.discovery.pluginRef -- both of those are imprecise shorthand for the same real, more deeply nested path. Includes a COMPLETE WORKING CODE SKELETON for writing a from-scratch custom discovery plugin (section Writing a custom discovery plugin): implement fwkdl.EndpointDiscovery, define a PluginType constant, a Factory(name string, params *json.Decoder, _ fwkplugin.Handle) (fwkplugin.Plugin, error) function, a TypedName() accessor, and a Start(ctx, notifier) method that upserts known endpoints then blocks on <-ctx.Done(); register the factory via fwkplugin.Register(myplugin.PluginType, myplugin.Factory) before calling Run(), then reference the plugin type string in the EndpointPickerConfig plugins list and point dataLayer.discovery.endpoints.pluginRef at it. Documents the exact EndpointMetadata struct fields discovery plugins populate: types.NamespacedName (ID), Name, Address, Port, MetricsHost (defaults to address:port if empty -- i.e. a discovery plugin CAN report a separate metrics endpoint per backend at the datastore level, even though the basic file-discovery YAML schema does not expose a metricsHost key directly), and Labels (map[string]string). Gives the full ASCII architecture diagram showing EndpointDiscovery plugin -> Upsert/Delete -> Datastore -> Scheduler -> x-gateway-destination-endpoint header -> Envoy ORIGINAL_DST cluster. Documents pod-readiness semantics specific to Kubernetes discovery (aggregate Ready condition, honors custom readinessGates, distinct from container-ready count shown in kubectl) -- notably a K8s-only mechanism a custom backend must replicate itself (e.g. only Upsert healthy endpoints) since file/custom discovery has no equivalent gate. Lists the SAME three Kubernetes-only inactive features as the file-discovery plugin README (InferenceModelRewrite, InferenceObjective, k8s-notification-source) but with more precise scope statements for each, and NOTABLY DOES NOT mention FlowControl as affected -- a discrepancy versus llm-d/llm-d's docs/infrastructure/no-kubernetes-deployment.md, which named 'InferenceObjective-driven FlowControl' as out of scope; this doc frames the same fact as InferenceObjective-driven per-request SLO objectives specifically, without describing FlowControl itself as disabled. Documents --pool-namespace resolution order (flag > NAMESPACE env var via K8s Downward API > default fallback) with an explicit warning that the fallback produces a Kubernetes-flavored label outside Kubernetes unless set explicitly. New validation detail not in the plugin README: duplicate endpoint names within the same namespace resolve with last-Upsert-wins, since the file is processed top-to-bottom. Confirms file-discovery's own backward-compatibility contract: when dataLayer.discovery.endpoints is absent, the EPP falls back to Kubernetes-based discovery -- no breaking change for existing deployments. Includes a complete, runnable end-to-end example: endpoints.yaml, EPP config.yaml (file-discovery + kv-cache-utilization-scorer + random-picker + single-profile-handler), the epp binary invocation, and a full Envoy YAML with the ext_proc filter (FULL_DUPLEX_STREAMED on all four body/trailer modes) and an ORIGINAL_DST cluster keyed on the x-gateway-destination-endpoint header. `evergreen` (2496 words)
- [[llm-d-routerdocsoperationsmd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-router-github]] — llm-d-router/docs/operations.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — THE load-bearing HA/leader-election doc for llm-d-router (discovered as a lead from peer-discovery.md; peer-discovery.md itself never mentions leader election). States the router supports two HA modes for the EPP: 'Fully Active-Active' and 'Active-Passive.' For Active-Passive, the DEFAULT/documented mechanism is Kubernetes-native and requires the Kubernetes API: 'Leader Election with Fail-Open: Uses Kubernetes coordination.k8s.io/Lease coordination so only the elected leader serves inference extension requests. Standby pods remain idle until acquiring the lease.' The dedicated 'Leader Election and Fail-Open' section confirms: 'In multi-replica deployments without priority routing (router.epp.replicas > 1), the router coordinates active-passive replicas using Kubernetes lease-based leader election: EPP replicas contend for a coordination.k8s.io/Lease. The --ha-enable-leader-election flag enables leader election in EPP (automatically injected by Helm when router.epp.replicas > 1). The elected leader responds to active gRPC extension requests on Port 9002, while standby replicas run idle.' No file-based, static-list, or gossip-based alternative lock/lease mechanism is documented anywhere for this leader-election path -- coordination.k8s.io/Lease (a Kubernetes API object) is the only lock primitive named. The ONLY two ways to avoid the Kubernetes Lease dependency are: (1) set ha-enable-leader-election: false to run 'Fully Active-Active' instead (each replica serves independently, no lock needed, but then relies on the separate, generically pluggable peer-discovery/state-sync mechanism from peer-discovery.md for any needed cross-replica state), or (2) use Envoy 'Priority Routing' (router.proxy.mode: service) as an alternative active-passive failover mechanism that does NOT use the Lease API at all, instead relying on 'EPP pods run as a StatefulSet with a headless Service' plus Envoy Outlier Detection / gRPC health checks for sub-second failover 'without lease expiration delays' -- but this still requires the EPP pods to run as a Kubernetes StatefulSet with a headless Service, so it substitutes one Kubernetes primitive (StatefulSet + Service + Envoy health checks) for another (Lease API) rather than removing the Kubernetes dependency. Also gives Active-Active throughput scaling factors (2 replicas=2.0x, 3=2.7x, 4=3.5x) and warns Active-Active degrades approximate prefix-cache hit rates because 'EPP replicas do not share prefix state.' `evergreen` (2386 words)

Implementations are registered in the plugin registry and selected by name. When omitted, peer discovery is disabled and no peer set is maintained.' No built-in plugin implementations (no 'kubernetes', 'file', or 'static' plugin type) are named anywhere in the doc -- the only example given is a hand-written custom plugin (MyPeerDiscovery) that a developer would author and register via fwkplugin.Register. The PeerNotifier is explicitly 'not goroutine-safe' and requires all Upsert/Delete calls to come from a single goroutine in order, but the doc gives no reconciliation or consistency mechanism for divergence across replicas using file/custom discovery sources -- that topic is not addressed here. Does NOT mention Kubernetes, Lease, coordination.k8s.io, or leader election anywhere; that mechanism lives in a sibling doc (operations.md), fetched separately. `evergreen` (951 words)

- [[llm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkinterfacedatalayerdiscoverygo-at-main-llm-dllm-d-rout]] — llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer/discovery.go at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE: full Go source of pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer/discovery.go on llm-d/llm-d-router main branch (Copyright 2025 The Kubernetes Authors, Apache 2.0; fetched Sep 11 2026, exact commit not pinned since URL targets main -- treat API shape as current-as-of-fetch, subject to change). Defines the exact EndpointDiscovery plugin contract referenced by the no-Kubernetes blog, with fuller doc comments than the blog quoted: type EndpointDiscovery interface { fwkplugin.Plugin; Start(ctx context.Context, notifier DiscoveryNotifier) error; Ready() <-chan struct{} }. Comment states implementations are "registered in the plugin registry and selected via EndpointPickerConfig.discovery.pluginRef" (note: the blog post phrased the equivalent config key as dataLayer.discovery.pluginRef -- a possible naming discrepancy between the source comment and blog prose that should be verified against the actual EndpointPickerConfig schema/example before being treated as authoritative). Documents explicit implementation requirements: Start SHOULD enumerate all currently known endpoints via notifier.Upsert before entering its watch loop (implementations with a guaranteed-no-missed-events watch, e.g. Kubernetes list+watch, MAY fold initial enumeration into the watch sequence instead); Ready()s channel closes at most once per Start call, only after a successful initial sync, and "zero endpoints is a valid outcome." Defines DiscoveryNotifier as NOT goroutine-safe -- "All calls must be made sequentially from a single goroutine" -- with a strict ordering contract: "the datastore processes Upsert and Delete calls in the order they are received... an Upsert followed by a Delete for the same endpoint must arrive in that order, or the endpoint will be incorrectly left in the datastore." Also defines a helper DiscoveryEndpointStore narrow interface (EndpointUpsert(ctx, *EndpointMetadata), EndpointDelete(id types.NamespacedName)) and NewDiscoveryNotifier(store DiscoveryEndpointStore) DiscoveryNotifier constructor that adapts any conforming store into a DiscoveryNotifier -- this is the concrete extension point a custom backend integration would implement against. EndpointDiscovery embeds fwkplugin.Plugin (from pkg/epp/framework/interface/plugin), meaning a custom discovery plugin must also satisfy whatever base Plugin interface that package defines (not yet independently confirmed in this note). `evergreen` (1436 words)

- [[llm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkpluginsreadmemd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-router-github]] — llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/plugins/README.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — llm-d-router pkg/epp/framework/plugins/README.md (fetched Sep 2026, latest commit 950c9636, Aug 25 2026, 'consolidate discovery plugins under a single discovery section'). Two findings directly answer the router-customization question. (1) Plugin registration and stability: every plugin gets a Stability Level (Alpha/Beta/Stable) assigned at registration, and 'cmd/epp/runner/runner.go is the single source of truth for in-tree plugin stability' -- confirming registration is compile-time Go code, gated by CLI flag for Alpha plugins (--allow-experimental-plugins; EPP fails initialization if an Alpha plugin is configured without the flag). All in-tree plugins are currently Alpha or Beta; none are Stable pre-1.0. (2) Multi-cluster variants are a SHIPPING, DOCUMENTED PRECEDENT for exactly the non-pod-backend use case: a cluster-scoped EPP whose 'candidate endpoints are clusters' rather than pods, using multicluster-kv-cache-utilization-scorer, multicluster-queue-scorer, multicluster-session-affinity-filter, multicluster-prefix-cache-scorer (scoring/filtering variants), multicluster-approx-prefix-cache-producer (data producer), and three DataLayer variants: multicluster-file-discovery (discovers peer 'clusters' as endpoints from a static file, e.g. /etc/epp/clusters.yaml), multicluster-metrics-data-source, and multicluster-metrics-extractor (scrape each cluster and write pool-aggregate metrics). Critically: 'clusters.yaml follows the same format as the pod file discovery, except an address may be a hostname' -- i.e. the discovery plugin's endpoint model already supports hostname/non-pod-IP addressing as a first-class alternative to Kubernetes pod discovery, driven entirely through the dataLayer.discovery.endpoints.pluginRef config field. Config example shows a complete non-pod-discovery EndpointPickerConfig using apiVersion llm-d.ai/v1alpha1. `evergreen` (702 words)
- [[llm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkpluginsdatalayerdiscoveryfile-at-main-llm-dllm-d-rout]] — llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/plugins/datalayer/discovery/file at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE: pkg/epp/framework/plugins/datalayer/discovery/file/README.md on llm-d/llm-d-router main branch (fetched Sep 11 2026, unpinned to a commit -- treat as current-as-of-fetch). This is the authoritative, most detailed spec of the FileDiscovery plugin (Type: file-discovery, Interface: EndpointDiscovery), superseding the looser prose in the llm-d.ai blog. Key mechanics: on Start, the file is read once, each entry validated (address must be a parseable IP, port in [1,65535]) and applied via notifier.Upsert; a bad single entry is logged and skipped (non-fatal), but file-level problems (cannot open, cannot parse, size > 1 MiB) ABORT STARTUP. With watchFile: true, fsnotify Write/Create/Remove events trigger reload, explicitly handling atomic-rename and ConfigMap-style symlink swaps (which destroy the watched inode) by re-attaching the watcher; endpoints present in the old load but absent from the new one are removed via notifier.Delete. Ready() closes only after the first successful load. GIVES THE EXACT FILE SCHEMA: endpoints: - name (required, unique in file), namespace (optional, defaults "default"), address (required, must be a valid IPv4 address -- hostnames NOT resolved, IPv6 NOT supported), port (required, string, integer 1-65535), labels (optional key/value map). CONFIG PATH DISCREPANCY (three-way, flag for the report): this README places the selector at dataLayer.discovery.endpoints.pluginRef (shown in a worked example alongside a plugins: - type: file-discovery entry) -- differing from BOTH the blog's dataLayer.discovery.pluginRef AND the discovery.go source comment's EndpointPickerConfig.discovery.pluginRef; the discovery/endpoints nesting in this README is the most concrete and should be treated as most authoritative since it comes with a full worked example, but all three should be verified against a live default-plugins.yaml before being presented as certain. STATED LIMITATIONS (verbatim, critical for a customization plan): endpoints file capped at 1 MiB; address must be literal IPv4 only; metrics are scraped from the same address:port that serves inference (no separate metrics endpoint supported); and -- new information not in the blog -- 'File-discovery mode runs the EPP without a Kubernetes controller manager, so several K8s-only features are inactive: the InferenceModelRewrite and InferenceObjective reconcilers do not run, and any k8s-notification-source plugin in the data layer config will not bind. The runner emits a startup log naming the inactive features.' Also documents a previously-undocumented MULTI-CLUSTER VARIANT: multicluster-file-discovery, a cluster-scoped plugin that discovers peer CLUSTERS (not pods) as endpoints reached via gateway host; unlike the pod variant, address may be an RFC-1123 hostname OR an IP (not IPv4-only), and a peer's pool metrics can be scraped from a different host/port via metricsAddress/metricsPort labels. `evergreen` (3077 words)

Contains the assertion 'var _ fwkdl.EndpointExtractor = &Producer{}' — Producer implements the ROUTER's own fwkdl.EndpointExtractor interface (package github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/interface/datalayer), confirming this is the router-level data-layer extractor pattern, not a direct call into the llm-d-kv-cache library's Kubernetes PodDiscoveryConfig reconciler. Extract(ctx, event fwkdl.EndpointEvent) is called for endpoint lifecycle events from the endpoint-notification-source: on fwkdl.EventAddOrUpdate it calls p.ensureSubscriber(meta), on fwkdl.EventDelete it calls p.subscribersManager.RemoveSubscriber(ctx, endpointKey) and clears index entries keyed 'address:port'. Two important findings: (1) the whole Extract() short-circuits to a no-op unless BOTH p.kvEventsConfig.DiscoverPods is true AND p.kvEventsConfig.PodDiscoveryConfig != nil — PodDiscoveryConfig here supplies only config values (SocketPort, EffectiveReplayPort) for constructing the ZMQ dial address (SocketPort + meta.GetRankIndex() for per-DP-rank port offsetting), it is NOT itself the Kubernetes reconciler; (2) the subscription identity key is endpointKey := meta.ID.String() (EndpointMetadata.ID, not a literal address:port string), while the ZMQ dial target is separately built from meta.Address + computed port. Since Extract is driven purely by fwkdl.EndpointEvent/EndpointMetadata supplied by whatever EndpointSource feeds endpoint-notification-source, this plugin is backend-agnostic as long as a non-Kubernetes EndpointDiscovery plugin (e.g. file-discovery) populates EndpointMetadata.ID/Address/Port/RankIndex and fires EventAddOrUpdate/EventDelete through that same data-layer path. `evergreen` (1105 words)

- [[llm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkpluginsrequesthandlingparsersreadmemd-at-main-llm-dll]] — llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/plugins/requesthandling/parsers/README.md at main · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — Upstream README (llm-d-router pkg/epp/framework/plugins/requesthandling/parsers, latest commit ebab6879, Aug 22 2026, PR #2507) documenting the request-handling parser-plugin pattern used for prefix-cache-aware scheduling and usage tracking. Parsers registered: openai-parser, anthropic-parser, vllmgrpc-parser, vllmhttp-parser (handles vLLMs non-OpenAI /inference/v1/generate path only), sglanghttp-parser (SGLang native /generate), vertexai-parser (Vertex AI PredictionService/ChatCompletions gRPC; unsupported Vertex APIs pass through unparsed to a random endpoint), and passthrough-parser (model-agnostic, claims no paths, must be listed last, forwards unparsed traffic without payload-derived scoring signals such as prefix-cache-scorer). Default parser list (if none specified) is openai-parser, anthropic-parser, vllmhttp-parser, passthrough-parser. Resolution uses longest-suffix path matching against each parsers claimed paths; registration/matching stops at the first parser claiming no paths, so any parser after a no-path fallback is skipped -- an explicit requestHandler.parsers list with no trailing fallback will 400-reject any path no listed parser claims, whereas relying on the default lists trailing passthrough-parser routes unclaimed paths through unparsed instead of rejecting. Gives three annotated EndpointPickerConfig YAML examples (vllmgrpc-only; vllmhttp+openai; openai+anthropic) showing how to register plugins and reference them in requestHandler.parsers. Directly informs plugin-registration pattern applicable to writing a custom EndpointDiscovery-style plugin: plugins are declared in a top-level plugins block with name+type, then referenced by name elsewhere in the EndpointPickerConfig. `evergreen` (830 words)

- [[llm-ddocsoperationsdisaggregationvllmmd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/docs/operations/disaggregation/vllm.md at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — llm-d GitHub ops doc (docs/operations/disaggregation/vllm.md, updated Aug 11 2026) covering NIXL-connector-specific operational complexity for P/D disaggregated serving. Dynamic Connections: a NIXL Handshake between decode and prefill instances costs ~5s and is done once per pair (subsequent requests reuse it); llm-d uses a 'dynamic lazy' rollout avoiding a centralized bootstrap server. Sequence: prefill runs, returns KVTransferParams (remote_host/remote_port/remote_request_id/remote_kv_blocks) to the routing proxy, which forwards to decode; decode spawns a background thread to fetch NIXLMetadata via ZMQ and bootstrap the RDMA connection (non-blocking), then issues a NIXL READ to pull KV blocks via RDMA. New P/D replicas are auto-discovered via standard Kubernetes InferencePool selectors once Running, no specialized service-discovery plane needed. Known NIXL Connector Issues and Limitations section documents two concrete gaps with source citations: (1) Prefill TP > Decode TP is unsupported for non-MLA/non-Mamba models -- NixlConnector only supports the fan-out direction (decode TP >= prefill TP); this is a deliberate assertion guard in vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py (_validate_remote_agent_handshake), identical since at least v0.25.0, and violating it crashes the decode engine with an AssertionError (sometimes masked as an unrelated IndexError from _handle_failed_transfer). (2) Stale NIXL agent cache after a prefill pod restart can segfault decode or silently serve against a dead engine for up to engine_ttl (default up to 1 hour) -- tracked as open bug vllm-project/vllm#49238; when it doesn't segfault, decode's RDMA read fails with NIXL_ERR_BACKEND and vLLM applies kv_load_failure_policy=recompute, silently falling back to local prefill recompute with only an error log line, invisible to the client. Recommends proactively restarting decode replicas connected to a restarted prefill pod rather than relying on TTL expiry. `evergreen` (2832 words)
- [[llm-ddocsoperationsobservabilitypromqlmd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/docs/operations/observability/promql.md at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — llm-d GitHub PromQL Query Reference (docs/operations/observability/promql.md, updated Aug 31 2026 via PR #2368 adding NIXL transfer metrics). Ready-to-use Prometheus queries organized in tiers. KV-cache/prefix-cache-specific queries: KV Cache Utilization = avg by(pod, model_name) (vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc) (SGLang equivalent: sglang_token_usage); Prefix cache hit rate = sum(rate(vllm:prefix_cache_hits_total[5m])) / sum(rate(vllm:prefix_cache_queries_total[5m])); EPP prefix indexer size = llm_d_epp_prefix_indexer_size; EPP prefix hit ratio P90 = histogram_quantile(0.90, sum by(le) (rate(llm_d_epp_prefix_indexer_hit_ratio_bucket[5m]))). P/D disaggregation queries: Disaggregation decision ratio = sum(rate(llm_d_epp_disagg_decision_total{decision_type="prefill-decode"}[5m])) / sum(rate(llm_d_epp_disagg_decision_total[5m])); Decode KV cache utilization scoped to decode pods only via pod=~".*decode.*" label match. NIXL transfer metrics (new in this revision): average/P95 NIXL transfer time from vllm:nixl_xfer_time_seconds_sum/count and _bucket; average transfer size and data volume from vllm:nixl_bytes_transferred_sum/count; failed transfer and failed notification counters (vllm:nixl_num_failed_transfers, vllm:nixl_num_failed_notifications); expired prefill KV requests (vllm:nixl_num_kv_expired_reqs). Explicit caveat: NIXL histogram observations are pooled across tensor-parallel ranks, so their counts/average sizes describe rank-level transfer observations, not inference requests or whole-request KV cache sizes. `evergreen` (957 words)

- [[llm-dguidesp2p-kv-cache-sharing-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/guides/p2p-kv-cache-sharing at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — Primary llm-d GitHub well-lit-path guide (Experimental) for P2P KV cache sharing, deploying openai/gpt-oss-120b with CPU-to-CPU NIXL (UCX/RDMA) pulls between peer vLLM pods. Documents the full production mechanism: the vLLM OffloadingConnector runs a P2P secondary CPU tier on every pod (each pod is both puller and source); the llm-d Router's precise KV-event-fed prefix index tracks which pods hold which prefix blocks; the p2p-source-producer plugin selects a CPU-tier source from peers within one index block of the largest cached prefix (weighted to avoid concentrating pulls on a queued source) and sets the KV-source header only when that source leads the computing pod by at least minCachedTokenDelta tokens; the routing sidecar injects kv_transfer_params.remote_kv_source and the engine pulls over NIXL — ordinary misses fall back to normal recompute. Provides decision guidance on when the pull is worth using: affinity+P2P (shipped default) barely changes throughput over affinity alone since the pull rarely fires; load-aware+P2P is what makes deliberate load-scattering affordable, delivering +143% sustained rate on a uniform pool and +224% with zero client timeouts on a hot set, and +35% throughput / 1.5x better p99 TTFT than affinity-warm on a document-Q&A workload (128 concurrent multi-turn sessions pinned to owner pods); P/D+P2P recovers KV that no placement decision could make local, delivering the guide's largest measured effect — 6.3x median TTFT and +50% throughput on a 2-prefill+4-decode Qwen3-30B rig when the prefill leg pulls decode-generated session history. Measured single-request prefill-latency deltas on gpt-oss-120b/H200 with rdma/ib show the pull winning at every tested length (2,048 tokens: -55.8% to 49,152 tokens: -88.2%), setting minCachedTokenDelta=2048 as the reference threshold (2,048 on this RDMA testbed vs. 12,288 on the wide-EP GLM-5.2 testbed). Extensive best-practices and troubleshooting sections cover block-size/PYTHONHASHSEED consistency requirements, KV-events topic/port matching, TP-matching between peers, CPU-tier sizing (recommended 2x per-pod GPU KV cache as a working default), and a P/D variant using MultiConnector (NIXL for P/D transfer + OffloadingConnector for the CPU tier and P2P listener). `evergreen` (5040 words)
- [[llm-dguidespd-disaggregationreadmemd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/guides/pd-disaggregation/README.md at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — llm-d GitHub guide (Sep 2026, guides/pd-disaggregation/README.md) for deploying openai/gpt-oss-120b with P/D disaggregation: 8 TP=1 prefill instances + 2 TP=4 decode instances. Cites arXiv:2506.05508 for the throughput/interactivity tradeoff claim that P/D disaggregation lowers inter-token latency (ITL) by removing prefill interference on decode. Documents two supported KV-transfer backends configured via vLLM's --kv-transfer-config: NixlConnector (default, UCX over RDMA/TCP, supports heterogeneous TP across P/D) and MooncakeConnector (cks-mooncake overlay, RDMA via Mooncake Transfer Engine, REQUIRES identical tensor-parallel-size on prefill and decode; kv_role=kv_producer on prefill / kv_consumer on decode; VLLM_MOONCAKE_ABORT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT defaults to 480s before a prefiller releases KV cache if the decoder doesn't acknowledge). Flags a known NixlConnector limitation around TP-ratio direction and stale agent caching after prefill pod restarts. Observability & Troubleshooting section gives concrete PromQL-backed thresholds: decode KV-cache utilization (vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc) above ~0.9 causes preemption/rejection and is usually the real scaling bottleneck (not prefill); llm_d_epp_pd_decision_total ratio drifting toward 0 signals requests falling back to aggregated (non-disaggregated) serving; diagnostic table maps TTFT regression -> prefill/KV-transfer stall, ITL regression -> decode bottleneck, both-pools-idle-but-high-latency -> routing-layer problem (check EPP scheduler e2e latency). `evergreen` (4309 words)
- [[llm-dguidesprecise-prefix-cache-routingreadmemd-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/guides/precise-prefix-cache-routing/README.md at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — llm-d GitHub guide (guides/precise-prefix-cache-routing/README.md, commit cab4b7d, Aug 2026) is the authoritative, current-HEAD spec for precise KV-cache-aware routing and answers the endpoint-identity/discovery questions directly. Each vLLM/SGLang pod is launched with '--kv-events-config {...,"publisher":"zmq","endpoint":"$(KV_EVENTS_ENDPOINT)","topic":"kv@$(POD_IP):$(POD_PORT)@<model>"}' and KV_EVENTS_ENDPOINT=tcp://*:5556 — i.e. the pod BINDS its own ZMQ PUB socket and the ZMQ topic is keyed by literal Pod IP:port plus model name, not by Kubernetes pod name/UID. The GPU vLLM backend (v0.26.0+) additionally binds a ZMQ ROUTER replay socket on port 5559 and retains the last 10,000 batches in an in-memory replay buffer for index recovery after a router restart. Router-side: 'pod-discovery (kvEventsConfig.discoverPods: true) registers the precise-prefix-cache-producer as an extractor on the data-layer endpoint-notification-source, so each router replica installs a ZMQ subscriber per model server pod independently' — i.e. discovery is push-registration-then-pull-connect: the router discovers pod IPs (implicitly via Kubernetes endpoint/pod watch given 'discoverPods') and then each EPP replica opens its own outbound ZMQ SUB connection to every discovered pod's bound PUB endpoint. All replicas converge to the same index independently, which the doc calls 'HA-safe... each replica converges independently via pod-discovery.' Tokenization for exact matching goes through a separate render Service that fronts the model-server pods' vLLM '/v1/*/render' endpoint (not through the ZMQ path) — render pods deliberately omit the llm-d.ai/guide label so the EPP does not try to subscribe to a nonexistent KV-event socket on them, confirming the EPP treats 'routable model server' status (and hence ZMQ-subscription eligibility) as driven by that Kubernetes label/selector rather than by an independent config list. States router runs single-replica by default because in-flight token load accounting is local to each EPP process (non-HA state), while the KV index itself is HA-safe across multiple router replicas. `evergreen` (2752 words)
- [[llm-dguidespredicted-latency-routing-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/guides/predicted-latency-routing at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — LONG (6188 words) upstream operator guide (llm-d/llm-d, guides/predicted-latency-routing, README last commit Sep 9 2026) for deploying predicted-latency-based scheduling. Entirely Kubernetes/Helm-native: prerequisites include kubectl, cloning the llm-d repo, applying Gateway API Inference Extension CRDs via kubectl apply, creating a namespace and an HF-token Secret, and deploying via `helm install` against either a Standalone (EPP + Envoy sidecar in one pod/chart, no Kubernetes Gateway object) or Gateway (EPP behind a Kubernetes Gateway/HTTPRoute, requires provider.name=gke|agentgateway|istio) topology -- both are k8s Helm charts, so "standalone mode" here means "no Gateway API object", not "no Kubernetes". States repeatedly and explicitly for every backend variant (GPU/vLLM, TPU, P/D-disaggregated, multimodal, Intel XPU) that "the latency predictor is EPP-side only, so the model server needs no predictor-specific changes" -- i.e., the training/prediction sidecars attach to the router/EPP deployment, never to model-server pods. Composes with P/D disaggregation (prefill scored on predicted TTFT, decode on predicted TPOT, requires streamingMode:true and stream:true on every client request since TPOT training samples come from streamed inter-token gaps) and with multimodal serving (keeps a token-producer that turns images into an estimated token count for the affinity filter and predictor features). Flags OpenShift support for this guide as "currently not reliable as-is" because the latency-predictor sidecars may need OpenShift-specific runtime adjustments beyond the shipped manifests -- prefer GKE or CoreWeave. Verification is via Prometheus metrics: llm_d_epp_request_ttft_prediction_duration_seconds (nonzero = predictor sidecar is being called), llm_d_epp_request_predicted_ttft_seconds vs llm_d_epp_request_ttft_seconds (should converge within a few percent after warmup), and llm_d_epp_request_slo_violation_total. Benchmarking uses the llmdbenchmark CLI, which auto-deploys a harness pod (llmdbench-harness-launcher) into the namespace -- another Kubernetes-Job-shaped tooling dependency in the reference workflow. `evergreen` (6198 words)
- [[llm-dguidesrecipesroutercalibration-at-main-llm-dllm-d-github]] — llm-d/guides/recipes/router/calibration at main · llm-d/llm-d · GitHub — Primary llm-d GitHub source (guides/recipes/router/calibration README) documenting the exact procedure and tooling for calibrating the two hardware-dependent routing constants used by llm-d's router plugins. (1) peakPrefillThroughput, used by the prefix-cache-affinity-filter to estimate per-endpoint TTFT from in-flight load: measured by calibrate.sh, which runs a Kubernetes Job sending warmup + measurement requests of exactly CHUNK_SIZE random token IDs (guaranteed cache misses) and computes peakPrefillThroughput = CHUNK_SIZE / median(TTFT) in tokens/sec; CHUNK_SIZE must match vLLM's --max-num-batched-tokens. The plugin default (15928) is calibrated for Qwen 32B on H100 80GB (TP=2); the agentic-serving guide ships 16444 for Qwen3-Coder-480B-FP8 on TPU v7x. A shared configuration-matrix.md tracks reference values by (model, accelerator). (2) minCachedTokenDelta, used by the p2p-source-producer to decide when a peer's cached prefix lead is large enough to justify a pull over recompute: measured by calibrate-min-cached-token-delta.sh, which seeds a fresh random-token prompt on a source pod and times the consumer pod serving it with and without kv_transfer_params.remote_kv_source (mesh pre-warmed to exclude one-time session-establishment cost), printing the smallest tested length where the pull won. Both calibration values are explicitly stated to be model-, hardware- and transport-specific and must be re-measured, not transplanted, when any of those change. `evergreen` (3268 words)

The author's own prediction ('llm-d will become the standard way to serve LLMs on Kubernetes within 18 months') and performance claims ('I have seen 2-3x improvement in tokens per second per GPU in similar architectures') are first-person opinion/anecdote from the consultant, not sourced to llm-d or a named production deployer -- flag as unverified anecdotal evidence, not a confirmed production claim. `review` (964 words)
- [[llm-d-intelligent-inference-at-scale-beyond-what-more-gpus-can-deliver-by-yakov]] — llm-d: Intelligent Inference at Scale — Beyond What More GPUs Can Deliver | by Yakov Beder | Medium — Deep technical walkthrough (dated Apr 9 2026, llm-d v0.6.0) of the llm-d Endpoint Picker (EPP): 4-step Discover->Filter->Score->Select cycle, default scoring formula final_score = prefix_score*3 + kv_utilization_score*2 + queue_score*2, and the full v0.7.1 plugin catalog (handlers: single-profile-handler, disagg-profile-handler, disagg-headers-handler; filters: decode-filter/prefill-filter/encode-filter keyed on llm-d.ai/role labels, by-label, by-label-selector; scorers: prefix-cache-scorer, precise-prefix-cache-scorer, queue-scorer, kv-cache-utilization-scorer, load-aware-scorer, active-request-scorer, session-affinity-scorer, no-hit-lru-scorer; deciders: prefix-based-pd-decider; pickers: max-score-picker, random-picker). Documents the llm-d-kv-cache Indexer's Read Path (EPP queries KV-Block Index) and Write Path (vLLM pods emit KVEvents via ZMQ), and the 3-tier cache hierarchy (GPU HBM hot / CPU DRAM warm / SSD-Lustre-LMCache cold). Reports 57x TTFT improvement and 2x throughput on 8 pods/16 H100s vs round-robin, and load-aware scoring lifting success rate from ~55% to 100% at high QPS. `evergreen` (2779 words)
- [[llm-d-kubernetes-native-distributed-inference-at-scale-llm-d]] — llm-d: Kubernetes-native Distributed Inference at Scale | llm-d — llm-d's founding design proposal (docs v0.7, dated to the project's origin before terminology evolved: 'inference scheduler' = today's llm-d Router). States llm-d's explicit Non-Goals: 'Prioritize non-Transformer model architectures (initially)', 'Fork upstream repositories or carry unmerged upstream changes', and 'Control the exact configuration of the end-user vLLM deployments' -- i.e. llm-d is deliberately NOT an opinionated platform and NOT an engine. Goals state it 'Is not opinionated about model server deployment orchestration and model server lifecycle.' The Alternatives section is the single most valuable passage for layer-boundary analysis: it names four alternatives and states exactly how llm-d differs from each. Vs NVIDIA Dynamo: llm-d makes the P/D disaggregation decision inside the scheduler (not an async queue/Distributed Runtime) and separates in-memory from durable prefix cache tiers rather than a unified KV Block Manager API. Vs AIBrix: llm-d 'prefers to adapt serving to existing user infrastructure vs. providing an opinionated serving platform' and prioritizes standardizing Gateway-ecosystem APIs over AIBrix's integrated approach. Vs production-stack (UChicago LMCache team's project, described as 'the easiest way to deploy vLLM on Kubernetes'): llm-d targets large-scale production serving with users expected to have strong opinions about surrounding infra. Vs KServe: llm-d 'does not orchestrate inference workloads directly' and 'does not attempt to solve problems related to traditional ML serving or models that consume less than 1 accelerator' -- KServe is the broader platform, llm-d plugs capabilities (LLMInferenceService CRD) into it. Documents the pre-llm-d capability gap directly: 'the key techniques enabling that scale are broadly understood but sparsely implemented and yield high operational toil' -- naming tiered prefix cache, disaggregated serving, LLM-optimized load balancing, and autoscaling as the four high-scale techniques platform teams previously had to build ad hoc. Confirms NIXL (via ai-dynamo project) as the shared KV-transfer dependency across llm-d, Dynamo, and AIBrix brixbench testing. `evergreen` (1864 words)
- [[llm-d-kubernetes-native-distributed-inferencing-red-hat-developer]] — llm-d: Kubernetes-native distributed inferencing | Red Hat Developer — Foundational May 20, 2025 Red Hat Developer post announcing the llm-d community (Clayton Coleman/Google, Carlos Costa/IBM), establishing llm-d's original design principles: Operationalizability (native Kubernetes integration via Inference Gateway API), Flexibility (cross-platform NVIDIA/TPU/AMD/Intel, extensible composable layers), Performance (disaggregation + prefix-aware routing). Names the three-layer architecture -- vLLM (inference engine), Kubernetes (orchestration), Inference Gateway/IGW (Gateway API extension implementing the Endpoint Picker Protocol/EPP) -- and states explicitly that 'Advanced teams can also tweak or implement their own scorers and filterers to further customize for their use cases, while still benefiting from upcoming operational features in the inference gateway, like flow control and latency-aware balancing,' confirming router customization was a first-class design goal from inception. Documents the pluggable vLLM KV Connector API used for both disaggregated P/D serving (via NVIDIA NIXL transport) and disaggregated prefix caching (independent host/disk offload vs shared caching with global indexing, e.g. LMCache). Reports early benchmark results on 2x NVIDIA 8xH100 nodes: llm-d achieves ~3x lower mean TTFT at 4 QPS, ~50% higher QPS at same SLO, and 2x baseline QPS under SLO constraints, tested against LlaMA 4 Scout FP8 and Llama 3.1 70B FP16 configurations. `evergreen` (2549 words)
- [[lm-cachemd]] — lm-cache.md — NVIDIA Dynamo's LMCache integration doc: documents LMCache as one optional KV-offload backend alongside Dynamo's own KV Block Manager, establishing that Dynamo does not depend on LMCache the way the vLLM production stack, llm-d and KServe do. `review` (1097 words)

- [[open-infra-index202502opensourceweekday_6_one_more_thing_deepseekv3r1_inference_]] — open-infra-index/202502OpenSourceWeek/day_6_one_more_thing_deepseekV3R1_inference_system_overview.md at main · deepseek-ai/open-infra-index · GitHub — DeepSeek's own first-party inference system design document (Open Source Week, Day 6, 'One More Thing', published Mar 1 2025 on GitHub), cited twice by llm-d's founding announcement as the exemplar of 'aggressive disaggregation to achieve remarkable performance at scale.' Confirms DeepSeek runs a fully custom, in-house inference system rather than adopting llm-d/AIBrix/vLLM-as-is: it is built around large-scale cross-node Expert Parallelism (EP) combined with prefill-decode disaggregation, using DIFFERENT parallelism degrees per phase -- Prefilling Phase uses Routed Expert EP32 / MLA-Shared-Expert DP32 (4 nodes per deployment unit, 9 routed experts + 1 shared expert per GPU), Decoding Phase uses Routed Expert EP144 / DP144 (18 nodes per unit, 2 routed experts + 1 shared expert per GPU). Documents a custom dual-batch computation-communication overlap strategy (microbatch splitting during prefill; a 5-stage pipeline subdividing the attention layer during decode) to hide cross-node EP communication cost behind computation -- engineering detail published separately at github.com/deepseek-ai/profile-data. Describes three custom load balancers (Prefill Load Balancer, Decode Load Balancer, Expert-Parallel Load Balancer) each targeting a distinct imbalance failure mode (core-attention computation skew, KVCache usage skew, per-expert computational hotspotting). This is evidence that at the extreme high end (DeepSeek-V3/R1 scale, MoE with 256 experts/8 active), operators build bespoke EP/DP orchestration and load-balancing logic rather than relying on the generic Kubernetes-native control planes (llm-d/AIBrix/Dynamo) covered elsewhere in this batch -- i.e. this document is the 'build your own' end of the spectrum those projects are trying to make unnecessary for less extreme workloads. NOTE per scope constraint: the document's H800 node-count and GPU-cost/revenue statistics (daily node occupancy, $/GPU-hour cost, token throughput per node) are hardware capacity/sizing content and are excluded from this run's claim extraction; only the software architecture (EP/DP parallelism strategy, overlap scheduling, load balancing) is treated as in-scope. `evergreen` (1123 words)

Plugins: always-disagg-pd-decider, disagg-profile-handler (parameters.deciders.prefill: always-disagg-pd-decider), prefill-filter, decode-filter, approx-prefix-cache-producer (maxPrefixTokensToMatch:131072), inflight-load-producer, prefix-cache-affinity-filter (peakPrefillThroughput:33821, measured through the full P/D + NIXL transfer path on 8x prefill TP=1 H200), token-load-scorer, active-request-scorer, max-score-picker. schedulingProfile 'prefill': prefill-filter -> prefix-cache-affinity-filter -> token-load-scorer -> max-score-picker (cache-affinity-aware). schedulingProfile 'decode': decode-filter -> active-request-scorer -> max-score-picker (pure load-based, no cache affinity/prefix producer involved in the decode leg). Forces replicas:1 for the same in-flight-accounting-locality reason as precise-prefix-cache-routing. `evergreen` (244 words)
- [[peergo]] — peer.go — PeerDiscovery/PeerMetadata/PeerNotifier interface definitions, sibling of peer-discovery.md's prose description but with more precise contracts. PeerMetadata{ID types.NamespacedName, Address string, Port string ("empty when the discovery source does not expose a port")}. PeerStore is 'intentionally separate from Datastore even if they have a similar shape: endpoints and peers have different consumers (scheduler vs. cross-replica syncer) and different lifecycles (bound to the InferencePool vs. the EPP deployment)' -- explicit confirmation InferencePool (a k8s CRD) is still the binding lifecycle anchor for endpoints even when peers are tracked separately. PeerDiscovery interface (Start/Ready, mirrors EndpointDiscovery) is 'selected via EndpointPickerConfig.dataLayer.discovery.peers.pluginRef.' PeerNotifier doc comment explicitly confirms file-based peer discovery is a real, named alternative to Kubernetes: 'It mirrors DiscoveryNotifier: the k8s reconciler and any file-based discovery feed the same sink.' PeerNotifier is NOT goroutine-safe with the same strict Upsert-before-Delete ordering contract as DiscoveryNotifier. No default/built-in PeerDiscovery implementation (kubernetes or file) ships in this file -- it is purely the interface; consistent with peer-discovery.md's finding that no built-in plugin type is named anywhere in the docs. `evergreen` (491 words)
- [[perfkvcache-match-prefixes-over-an-ordered-index-walk-by-nilig-pull-request-2680]] — perf(kvcache): match prefixes over an ordered index walk by nilig · Pull Request #2680 · llm-d/llm-d-router · GitHub — Merged PR #2680 (llm-d/llm-d-router, merged Sep 9, 2026 by vMaroon) is the actual fix for both the InMemoryIndex.Lookup atomicity bug (issue #2698) and a major performance rework of prefix-cache matching. It introduces kvblock.KeyWalker, an optional Index capability that visits requested keys IN ORDER and lends each key's records (with pod/tier ordinals) under that key's lock, replacing the old pattern where the matcher materialized the full Lookup result (copying every key's entries into a map before scoring). The precise-prefix matcher (Indexer.MatchBlockKeys) now walks when the backend supports KeyWalker and falls back to materialized Lookup for backends without it. Benchmarks on an AMD EPYC 9655P (16 CPUs, n=6): ScoreTokens at 240K tokens/96 pods dropped from 1,185.5ms/12.2GiB (original) to 68.9ms/90.7MiB (materialized baseline PR #2669) to 6.11ms/1.6MiB (this PR's walk) -- roughly 190x latency and 7,600x memory reduction end to end; the multi-rank shape (1,034 blocks, 40 endpoints) went from 551.1ms/4.6GiB to 85.6ms/111MiB to 1.56ms/418KiB. The rewritten Lookup also reads entries and their count in a single critical section under PodCache.mu via a new PodCache.filteredEntries method (closing #2698 without a separate patch). Telemetry semantics changed: score_tokens span attributes blocks_found and block_hit_ratio now report the longest CONTIGUOUS prefix one candidate endpoint holds (in block positions) instead of the count of distinct requested keys held; lookup_hits_total and max_pod_hit_count_total record this once per successful match under EnableMetrics rather than per direct Lookup call. Index key recency (LRU) is now refreshed once per read instead of once per key. A default of 10 pod entries per key applies when inMemoryConfig.podCacheSize is omitted or non-positive. `evergreen` (3734 words)

- [[plugingo]] — plugin.go — llm-d-router pkg/epp/framework/plugins/datalayer/discovery/file/plugin.go at main (fetched 2026-09-11): source of the file-discovery EndpointDiscovery plugin (PluginType 'file-discovery'). EndpointEntry{name, namespace (optional, defaults to 'default'), address, port (string), labels}; params{path, watchFile}. Factory(name, *json.Decoder, fwkplugin.Handle) (fwkplugin.Plugin, error) is the plugin-factory signature a custom discovery plugin would copy. Addresses must be IPv4 literals ('matching the pod discovery contract'), but the validator is injected at construction 'so a variant can loosen it' (the multicluster variant accepts hostnames). Each entry becomes EndpointMetadata{ID: types.NamespacedName{Name, Namespace}, Address, Port, MetricsHost: address:port, Labels} via notifier.Upsert; entries missing on reload are notifier.Delete'd. Implements StateDumper for sanitized debug dumps. `evergreen` (1216 words)

- [[pod_reconcilergo]] — pod_reconciler.go — llm-d-router's default Kubernetes Pod discovery (pkg/epp/controller/pod_reconciler.go) is a plain controller-runtime Reconciler, not a separate 'inference-pool-discovery' plugin (no such k8s discovery plugin exists in this repo as of Sep 2026 — the only pluggable EndpointDiscovery implementation is file-discovery, used to replace Kubernetes mode entirely). PodReconciler watches all corev1.Pod objects with an event predicate that admits a Pod only if Datastore.PoolLabelsMatch(pod.GetLabels()) is true (InferencePool.spec.selector label match). On reconcile, updateDatastore() gates entry into the routing datastore on exactly two conditions ANDed together: podutil.IsPodReady(pod) (aggregate corev1.PodReady condition == ConditionTrue, and pod.DeletionTimestamp.IsZero()) and PoolLabelsMatch(pod.Labels) again. If either fails, the pod is deleted from the datastore; otherwise Datastore.PodUpdateOrAddIfNotExist(ctx, pod) is called. There is no check anywhere in this reconcile path on pod.Status.PodIP emptiness/validity, pod.Spec.NodeName, or pod.Status.Phase. `evergreen` (332 words)
- [[precise-prefix-cache-routingvaluesyaml]] — precise-prefix-cache-routing.values.yaml — Actual shipped Helm values file (guides/precise-prefix-cache-routing/router/precise-prefix-cache-routing.values.yaml, llm-d/llm-d main) for the precise KV-cache-aware routing well-lit path. Uses precise-prefix-cache-producer (source comment: 'precise-prefix-cache-scorer is deprecated'), NOT approx-prefix-cache-producer, configured with blockSizeTokens:64, speculativeIndexing:true, kvEventsConfig{topicFilter:'kv@', concurrency:8, discoverPods:true, podDiscoveryConfig{socketPort:5556, replaySocketPort:5559}}. Also wires token-producer (HTTP tokenization against a render Service, modelName Qwen/Qwen3-32B), endpoint-notification-source, inflight-load-producer, prefix-cache-affinity-filter (parameterized with prefixMatchInfoProducerName: precise-prefix-cache-producer and peakPrefillThroughput:15926), and token-load-scorer. schedulingProfiles default plugins: prefix-cache-affinity-filter + token-load-scorer -- same two-plugin scheduling profile shape as optimized-baseline, differing only in which prefix producer feeds the affinity filter. Forces router.epp.replicas:1 (in-flight load accounting is per-EPP-process, not shared across replicas) with a documented pflag workaround to keep ha-enable-leader-election off. `evergreen` (488 words)
- [[prefixcacheaffinity-package-githubcomllm-dllm-d-routerpkgeppframeworkpluginssche]] — prefixcacheaffinity package - github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/plugins/scheduling/filter/prefixcacheaffinity - Go Packages — Godoc for llm-d-router's prefix-cache-affinity-filter plugin (package prefixcacheaffinity, github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router pkg/epp/framework/plugins/scheduling/filter/prefixcacheaffinity, v0.10.0). Documents the exported DefaultConfig struct verbatim: AffinityThreshold=0.80, ExplorationProbability=0, MaxTTFTPenaltyMs=18000, TTFTSource=prefillThroughput, PeakPrefillThroughput=15928 tokens/sec (calibrated for Qwen 32B on 2x H100 80GB TP=2 with vLLM 0.19, measured as prefill throughput of one unloaded max_num_batched_tokens=8192 chunk). Filter narrows candidates to endpoints with prefix cache score >= affinityThreshold ('sticky'); if best sticky endpoint's estimated TTFT (inFlightTokens/peakPrefillThroughput*1000) exceeds best non-sticky by more than maxTTFTPenaltyMs, stickiness breaks and all endpoints are reopened. Config example shows the plugin wired into a schedulingProfile named 'default'. `evergreen` (1319 words)
- [[proxygo]] — proxy.go — Go source (pkg/sidecar/proxy/proxy.go, llm-d-router@main), the pd-sidecar's Server type. Start() constructs the AllowlistValidator from s.config.EnableSSRFProtection/PoolGroup/InferencePoolNamespace/InferencePoolName -- so wiring the validator is unconditional but its actual watch-and-enforce behavior is a no-op unless SSRF protection is on. createProxyHandler/prefillerProxyHandler build an httputil.NewSingleHostReverseProxy for whatever host:port string it is given (from the x-prefiller-host-port header, already allowlist-checked or not upstream in chat_completions.go) with no additional host validation, DNS-rebinding protection, or network-layer restriction at this layer -- the reverse proxy will dial any resolvable host:port passed to it. `evergreen` (3032 words)
- [[registrygo]] — registry.go — pkg/epp/framework/interface/plugin/registry.go defines the actual plugin registry as PACKAGE-LEVEL EXPORTED globals: var Registry = map[string]FactoryFunc{} and var RegistryMetadata = map[string]PluginMetadata{}, mutated by the exported functions Register(pluginType, stability, factory), RegisterAsDefaultProducer(...), RegisterWithPluginDependencies(...), and RegisterDeprecated(...). Because Registry is an exported package-level map (not a private field on Runner, and not gated by any internal-only build tag), any Go package that imports github.com/llm-d/llm-d-router/pkg/epp/framework/interface/plugin can call plugin.Register(...) itself -- typically from an init() function in a separate plugin package -- and that entry becomes visible to loader.InstantiateAndConfigure regardless of whether it was registered by llm-d-router's own registerInTreePlugins() or by external code, since Go guarantees all package init() functions run before main() executes. This is the concrete mechanism that makes out-of-tree plugin registration technically possible without patching the router's Go source: a custom main.go can blank-import a third-party discovery-plugin package (registering via init()) and then call runner.NewRunner().Run(ctx), and the custom plugin type name becomes usable in dataLayer.discovery.endpoints.pluginRef. `evergreen` (658 words)

This resolver is unrelated to SSRF allowlisting -- it exists purely to keep peer IPs current across pod restarts, not to validate or restrict which hosts may be dialed. `evergreen` (1659 words)

However, plugin registration itself happens inside an UNEXPORTED method, func (r *Runner) registerInTreePlugins(), called automatically at the start of Run() before config parsing; this method only registers llm-d-router's own in-tree plugins (bylabel, sessionaffinityfilter, discoveryfile.PluginType/MultiClusterPluginType, saturation detectors, flowcontrol plugins, etc.) via fwkplugin.Register(Type, Stability, Factory) calls hardcoded in this file. - [[semantic-conventions-genaidocsgen-aigen-ai-metricsmd-at-main-open-telemetryseman]] — semantic-conventions-genai/docs/gen-ai/gen-ai-metrics.md at main · open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai · GitHub — OTel GenAI metrics spec (docs/gen-ai/gen-ai-metrics.md, 820 lines): document Status is explicitly 'Development' (pre-Experimental maturity), same as the spans doc — confirms the whole GenAI semconv surface is still unstable as of Oct 2026. Explicit disclaimer: 'These are initial Generative AI client metric instruments and attributes but more may be added in the future.' Defines four metric families: (1) client-side gen_ai.client.operation.duration (non-inference operations only — inference duration moved to a separate gen_ai.client.inference.duration per a recent refactor); (2) MODEL-SERVER metrics — gen_ai.server.request.duration, gen_ai.server.time_per_output_token, gen_ai.server.time_to_first_token — i.e. engine-level latency/TTFT/TPOT histograms with standard bucket boundaries [0.01s...81.92s]; (3) workflow metrics (gen_ai.invoke_workflow.duration); (4) agent and tool metrics (invoke_agent.duration/inference_calls/tool_calls, execute_tool.duration). Notably absent: no engine-internal metrics (KV-cache utilization, queue depth, batch/running-request counts, GPU occupancy) — the standardized surface stops at request-level duration/token-timing, which is consistent with inference engines like vLLM needing engine-native Prometheus exporters (and operators like Netflix needing custom proxies) for anything below that line. `review` (6148 words)
- [[semantic-conventions-genaidocsgen-aigen-ai-spansmd-at-main-open-telemetrysemanti]] — semantic-conventions-genai/docs/gen-ai/gen-ai-spans.md at main · open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai · GitHub — OTel GenAI spans spec (docs/gen-ai/gen-ai-spans.md, 1015 lines): document Status is explicitly 'Development' (per OTel's document-status taxonomy, a lower maturity tier than 'Experimental' — meaning breaking changes can land at any time), NOT stable. Defines span conventions for embeddings, execute-tool, and inference operations (inference spans/metrics are factored into a separate client-inference.md doc as of an Oct 6 2026 refactor). Attribute-level requirement levels are granular (Required / Conditionally Required / Recommended) covering gen_ai.operation.name, gen_ai.provider.name, error.type, request model name, server.port, response model name, server.address. Also documents three content-capture modes: full buffered content on span attributes, uploading content to external storage, and streaming-chunk capture. The gen_ai.provider.name attribute is explicitly designed to discriminate telemetry 'flavor' even when a client is configured against a proxy that transparently relays to a different upstream provider — directly relevant to gateway/proxy-layer tracing. `review` (7950 words)

- [[service_discoverypy]] — service_discovery.py — Primary source (vllm-project/production-stack src/vllm_router/service_discovery.py) for the ServiceDiscovery abstraction and StaticServiceDiscovery/K8s implementations -- independent reference design from the DeepWiki secondary summary already in the vault. ABSTRACT BASE (abc.ABCMeta): ServiceDiscovery requires only ONE abstract method -- get_endpoint_info() -> List[EndpointInfo] (returns currently-known healthy endpoints). Two methods have default/no-op implementations a backend may override: get_health() -> bool (default True) and close() -> None (default no-op). This is a minimal 3-method contract: get_endpoint_info, get_health, close. StaticServiceDiscovery: constructed with parallel lists (urls, models, aliases, model_labels, model_types) -- asserts len(urls)==len(models) at construction. It is NOT purely one-shot/immutable: get_endpoint_info() is called repeatedly by the router and re-filters based on self.unhealthy_endpoint_hashes, which IS refreshed on an interval -- if static_backend_health_checks=True, it spawns a background asyncio-loop-in-a-thread (start_health_check_task) that calls check_model_health() every static_backend_health_check_interval seconds (default 60s), actively probing each configured URL's health via utils.is_model_healthy() and updating unhealthy_endpoint_hashes; unhealthy endpoints are excluded from get_endpoint_info() results even though the URL/model list itself never changes. So: static TOPOLOGY (fixed URL/model list, set once at construction) but DYNAMIC HEALTH (periodic re-check filters which of those static endpoints are currently returned) -- a hybrid worth citing against the 'is static truly static' question. K8sPodIPServiceDiscovery and K8sServiceNameServiceDiscovery both use the official kubernetes watch.Watch() client in a background daemon thread (_watch_engines loops watch stream with configurable watcher_timeout_seconds, catches exceptions and retries after a 0.5s sleep -- i.e. its own ad hoc reconnect-on-error loop) and gate ADDED/MODIFIED events on is_pod_ready (container readiness AND not terminating) before adding an engine, mirroring llm-d-router's Ready-filtering requirement; DELETED and not-ready MODIFIED both route to _delete_engine. ServiceDiscoveryType enum also has EXTERNAL_ONLY (ExternalOnlyServiceDiscovery, a total no-op returning empty list / healthy=True) for external-provider-only deployments with no local backends. `evergreen` (4232 words)
- [[sglangpythonsglangsrtdisaggregationkv_eventspy-at-main-sgl-projectsglang-github]] — sglang/python/sglang/srt/disaggregation/kv_events.py at main · sgl-project/sglang · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE: SGLang production code (python/sglang/srt/disaggregation/kv_events.py, main branch) defining the wire-format msgspec structs for KV-cache events and the ZmqEventPublisher class. BlockStored struct fields: block_hashes: list[int], parent_block_hash: Optional[int], token_ids: list[int], block_size: int, lora_id: Optional[int], medium: Optional[str]. BlockRemoved struct fields: block_hashes: list[int], medium: Optional[str]. AllBlocksCleared is an empty struct. Crucially, BlockStored has NO extra_keys/multimodal field analogous to vLLM's extra_keys -- multimodal/salted differentiation is instead handled via a separate BlockStoredWithMetadata subtype carrying a BlockStoredMetadata.cache_salt: str field (deliberately kept out of the KVEventBatch tagged union so legacy typed consumers decode it under the base 'BlockStored' tag and silently ignore the trailing metadata). This is direct code confirmation of the corpus's known parity gap: SGLang's cache_salt/extra-metadata path is structurally separate from and not equivalent to vLLM's per-block extra_keys multimodal hash list, so a naive llm-d consumer reading only the base BlockStored tag would miss the salt/metadata -- consistent with 'SGLang KV-events currently omitting extra_keys so salted SGLang requests are precise-cache misses.'\n\nStorageMedium enum: GPU ('GPU', L1 device HBM), CPU ('CPU_PINNED', L2 host pinned memory), DISK ('DISK', L3 SSD/NVMe), EXTERNAL ('EXTERNAL', L4 shared/remote pool e.g. Mooncake) -- a 4-tier hierarchy vLLM's 2-tier (GPU/CPU) model does not expose.\n\nZmqEventPublisher is a 'Reliable PUB/ROUTER publisher with an in-memory replay buffer' -- endpoint is a PUB address (e.g. tcp://*:5557 to bind), replay_endpoint is an optional ROUTER address letting subscribers request missed batches by sequence number, buffer_steps controls replay depth, hwm sets the ZeroMQ high-water-mark, and there's a configurable topic string. The --kv-events-config CLI flag is confirmed live in this file's --load-publish-endpoint auto-port-packing logic, which reads kv_endpoint from --kv-events-config and requires 'a bindable wildcard-host tcp:// --kv-events-config endpoint' -- routers discover the load range 'through /server_info's kv_events block.' EventPublisher design explicitly supports DP attention: 'Each DP rank has its own EventPublisher' and 'Publishers annotate events with the dp rank.' `evergreen` (3810 words)
- [[sglangpythonsglangsrtmem_cacheeventspy-at-main-sgl-projectsglang-github]] — sglang/python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/events.py at main · sgl-project/sglang · GitHub — PRIMARY SOURCE: SGLang production code (python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/events.py, main branch, commit 0b415fa, Sep 12 2026) confirming SGLang natively implements BlockStored/BlockRemoved/AllBlocksCleared KV-cache placement events. Module docstring: 'Produces the BlockStored / BlockRemoved / AllBlocksCleared events consumed by KV-aware routers (e.g. dynamo).' -- note the named consumer in-code is NVIDIA Dynamo, not llm-d (llm-d's SGLangAdapter consumes the same wire schema per llm-d-kv-cache's architecture.md, but SGLang's own docstring doesn't credit llm-d specifically).\n\nKVCacheEventRecorder.record_store() builds one BlockStored per page_size-token chunk from the radix-tree node, computing block_hashes via hash_str_to_int64(event_hash_values[page_index]) where event_hash_values come from compute_node_hash_values()/compute_node_event_hash_values() in sglang.srt.mem_cache.utils -- an SGLang-internal hashing scheme, NOT vLLM's sha256/xxhash chain reused verbatim. parent_block_hash chains blocks together (root node contributes no link). BlockStoredWithMetadata carries an optional cache_salt (used for the 'extra_key'/salted-request differentiation) and BlockStoredMetadata; LoRA is represented via a lora_id field. record_remove() emits BlockRemoved carrying only block_hashes (no tokens), matching the vLLM event shape. Adjacent compatible events are coalesced in a queue (extending block_hashes/token_ids) 'while preserving ordering and the parent-linked store chains consumers use to rebuild the cache tree.' page_size is a recorder-level constructor parameter (not hardcoded), confirming SGLang's block/page granularity is configurable rather than a fixed built-in default matching vLLM's 16.\n\nThis is direct code evidence that SGLang's KV-event publishing is production reality (not aspirational), but the underlying block-hash VALUES are computed by SGLang's own hash_str_to_int64/compute_node_hash_values machinery, independent of vLLM's parent_hash+token_chunk sha256/xxhash chain -- consistent with llm-d's dual-key design (the indexer only needs token_ids from the wire payload, not engine-hash bit-compatibility). `evergreen` (1882 words)
- [[sglang_adaptergo]] — sglang_adapter.go — PRIMARY SOURCE: llm-d-router v0.10.0 Go source, pkg/kvevents/engineadapter/sglang_adapter.go. Decisive evidence for gap cc-1. The SGLangAdapter decodes SGLang's BlockStored event as a 9-field positional msgpack array (sglangBlockStoredFieldCount=9: tag+block_hashes+parent+tokens+block_size+lora_id+medium+lora_name+extra_keys) and DOES populate kvevents.BlockStoredEvent.LoraID, LoraName, and ExtraKeys from SGLang's wire format via convertBlockStoredEvent -- so lora_id/lora_name/extra_keys ARE parsed. However there is NO CacheSalt field anywhere in msgpackSGLangBlockStoredEvent and no entry in eventConverters (which only maps eventTagBlockStored, eventTagBlockRemoved, eventTagAllBlocksCleared) for a distinct 'BlockStoredWithMetadata' tag -- meaning SGLang's cache_salt, which per SGLang's own kv_events.py source lives in a separate BlockStoredMetadata struct deliberately kept out of the tagged union under a different tag, has no decode path in llm-d-router's SGLang adapter at v0.10.0. Comment explicitly cites 'sglang/srt/disaggregation/kv_events.py (BlockStored, BlockRemoved classes)' as the schema reference, confirming the adapter was written against SGLang's base BlockStored/BlockRemoved classes, not the metadata-carrying subtype. `evergreen` (959 words)
- [[sidecar-pd-multi-tier-decode-leg-is-dispatched-concurrently-with-prefill-chargin]] — sidecar: PD Multi Tier decode leg is dispatched concurrently with prefill, charging producer latency to the connector's load deadline · Issue #2438 · llm-d/llm-d-router — Closed llm-d-router GitHub issue #2438 (opened Aug 18 2026, closed Sep 5 2026 via PR #2451) documenting a KV-transfer race in the PD Multi Tier sidecar path (P2P/offloading connector, distinct from the default sequential nixlv2 path). handleP2P dispatched prefill and decode legs concurrently, so the decoder's FetchMsg could reach the prefiller before it had stored KV for that kv_request_id; the decoder then burned vLLM's hard-coded 30s _LOAD_TIMEOUT_S waiting, and under kv_load_failure_policy=recompute silently fell back to recomputing the prompt locally -- defeating the point of disaggregation. Measured on 8 prefill + 8 decode gpt-oss-120b (TP=1, vLLM v0.27.1, 16x H200): fetch_hit_immediate=0 in every sample; concurrent-dispatch supply delay mean 15.83s (max 29.92s, effectively clipped by the 30s deadline) versus sequential-dispatch mean 0.002s (max 0.234s); consumer load latency mean fell from 14.387s to 0.291s after the fix. 6,083 load timeouts logged against 10,800 requests before the fix. Fix: sequence the legs (await prefill completion, then dispatch decode, propagating prefill errors verbatim) to match the NIXL connector's ordering. Notes a related unresolved upstream vLLM defect (vllm-project/vllm#52808) where a producer already holding requested blocks supplies nothing, causing timeouts independent of dispatch ordering. `evergreen` (726 words)
- [[tlsgo]] — tls.go — Go source (pkg/sidecar/proxy/tls.go, llm-d-router@main): CreateSelfSignedTLSCertificate generates a 10-year self-signed RSA-4096 cert for the sidecar's own inbound HTTPS listener when --secure-serving is on and no --cert-path is given (ported from kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension's internal/tls). Outbound TLS to the prefill/decode/encoder hops is configured separately in proxy.go's newProxyTransport with a standard crypto/tls.Config (MinVersion TLS1.2, a fixed AEAD cipher suite list, InsecureSkipVerify only when --tls-insecure-skip-verify names that stage) -- ordinary Go TLS client verification against the target's certificate, with no Kubernetes CA or secret dependency. `evergreen` (260 words)
- [[v08-release-goals-mid-june-issue-1520-llm-dllm-d]] — v0.8 Release Goals (Mid-June) · Issue #1520 · llm-d/llm-d — Primary-source GitHub tracking issue #1520 ('v0.8 Release Goals (Mid-June)'), opened May 16 2026 by robertgshaw2-redhat, closed Jun 23 2026 as the v0.8.0 milestone completed (target Jun 21, code freeze Jun 14). This is the single most load-bearing source found for the non-Kubernetes router question: under 'sig-rl' it lists 'Non-Kubernetes deployment mode' (owner @ezrasilvera) linking the design proposal at github.com/llm-d/llm-d/blob/main/docs/proposals/non-kubernetes-mode.md, and enumerates the EXACT merged-PR implementation chain in llm-d-router:
1. llm-d-router#908 - 'feat: Add pluggable DiscoveryPlugin interface'
2. llm-d-router#1080 - 'file-discovery-1: add BackendUpsert/Delete interface methods and extract upsertEndpoint helper'
3. llm-d-router#1081 - 'filediscovery-2: add DiscoveryBackendStore interface, NewDiscoveryNotifier'
4. llm-d-router#1135 - 'file-discovery (3/4): add FileDiscovery plugin with YAML/JSON file loading and fsnotify watch'
5. llm-d-router#1154 - 'rename BackendUpsert/Delete and DiscoveryBackendStore to us...' (naming cleanup)
6. llm-d-router#1218 - 'FileDiscovery plugin (4/4) - wiring into the runner'
7. llm-d-router#1338 - 'docs(discovery): correct binary name, module path, Factory signature'
8. llm-d-router#1362 - 'fix: resolve gRPC size parameter omission in runWithFileDiscovery runner initialization'
9. llm-d-router#1368 - 'epp: wire FlowControl into file-discovery mode'
10. llm-d-router#1404 - 'fix(datalayer): dispatch endpoint update notifications'
This is a clean four-part sequence (file-discovery 1/4 through 4/4) building: a pluggable DiscoveryPlugin interface -> a DiscoveryBackendStore abstraction with BackendUpsert/Delete methods and a notifier -> a concrete FileDiscovery plugin that loads backend definitions from YAML/JSON and watches the file via fsnotify for live updates -> wiring the FileDiscovery plugin into the router's runner (binary entrypoint), followed by cleanup/bugfixes including making Flow Control (the admission-control feature) work in file-discovery mode and fixing endpoint-update notification dispatch in the data layer.

Also under sig-rl: a 'Non-Kubernetes deployment guide' merged as llm-d/llm-d PR #1618 ('Add no-kubernetes-deployment guide'); a companion issue #1659 '[Docs][RL] Guide: RL training loop with llm-d serving (no Kubernetes)'; and a related but distinct 'time-slicing platform PoC' proposal at docs/proposals/rl-time-slicing-platform.md (GPU time-sharing for RL rollout workers, tentative/unvalidated as of this issue). A May 19 2026 comment from contributor chcost summarizes sig-rl goals as: (1) Python scheduler integration with EPP for RL-optimized routing, (2) 'Non-K8s mode - Enables llm-d in Slurm/research labs without Kubernetes (file-based discovery)', (3) time-slicing GPU sharing, (4) deployment guide for an llm-d-rl controller with framework examples.

Other v0.8 goals of note for router/disaggregation context: sig-router lists 'finalize gaie migration' (#1528), a stateless Responses/Messages API (#1529), Flow Control graduation to production (#1530), and a multi-modal guide (#1531); sig-disaggregation lists NIXL TTL (#1522), NIXL request draining (#1523), a Mooncake connector (#1524), a MoRI connector (via #1470), and DP-aware WideEP scheduling graduation (#1521); sig-kv-disaggregation lists multi-tier CPU->storage offloading, HMA support, NIXL P/D + P2P backends, filesystem and object-store backends, and heterogeneous TP work, plus a P2P NIXL EC-Connector tracked at llm-d-kv-cache#602 and storage KV-cache indexing tracked at llm-d-kv-cache#520. `evergreen` (2179 words)
- [[vattention-dynamic-memory-management-for]] — vAttention: Dynamic Memory Management for — vAttention (Prabhu, Nayak, Mohan, Ramjee, Panwar — Microsoft Research India + IISc Bengaluru, ASPLOS'25, arXiv 2405.04437) is a direct, named technical critique of PagedAttention/vLLM's core design, not a complementary optimization. Central argument: PagedAttention conflates two separable problems — (1) eliminating KV-cache physical-memory fragmentation and (2) the virtual-memory layout of the cache — and solves both by making the KV cache non-contiguous in virtual memory, which the authors argue is an unforced design mistake. PagedAttention implements demand-paging logic in user space (duplicating what an OS MMU already does transparently), which the paper documents has three concrete costs, each with cited numbers: (a) it requires rewriting every attention kernel to dereference non-contiguous blocks — vLLM's own paged kernel is up to 2.8x slower than FlashAttention-2's kernel (Table 7), and new kernels lag: FlashAttention-3 and cuDNN-9's SDPA shipped on Hopper without PagedAttention support at all, and TensorRT-LLM's Python frontend lost >10% throughput using it; (b) it forces the serving framework to track and pass block-table virtual-address mappings into every kernel call, a form of redundant address translation; (c) measured runtime overhead: PagedAttention-based FlashAttention-2 prefill kernels are up to 37% slower and FlashInfer's up to 42% slower than their non-paged equivalents (Figure 2, Llama-3-8B/A100), attributed to 7-13% more executed instructions and register-spilling from cached page indices; vLLM's own paper (cited) already acknowledged its paged kernel was 20-26% slower than non-paged FasterTransformer. Block-size choice also matters: vLLM's paged decode kernel degrades up to 1.9x with block sizes of 64-128 due to L1 cache effects. vAttention's fix: decouple virtual and physical memory allocation via CUDA's low-level virtual memory management (VMM) APIs, keeping the KV cache virtually contiguous (so existing unmodified, non-paged attention kernels work out-of-the-box) while physical pages are still committed on demand. To make this performant the authors had to patch the open-source CUDA unified-memory driver to add 64KB page support (CUDA VMM natively only supports 2MB pages, which would otherwise cause its own fragmentation) and add LLM-specific latency hiding: overlapping allocation with compute, speculative ahead-of-time page allocation, and deferred reclamation. Evaluated against vLLM, FlashAttention-2, and FlashInfer on Yi-6B, Llama-3-8B, Yi-34B (1-2 A100s): vAttention improves decode throughput up to 1.99x over vLLM (using FlashAttention-2's non-paged kernel), and end-to-end serving throughput up to 1.18x-1.23x over PagedAttention-based FlashAttention-2/FlashInfer in long-context scenarios; portability payoff demonstrated directly — vAttention supports the newly-released FlashAttention-3 kernel out-of-the-box (FA3 shipped with zero PagedAttention support) for 1.26x-1.5x higher throughput versus paged FlashAttention-2. Acknowledgments confirm this is a live, contested design debate inside the vLLM community: two named GitHub contributors (izhuhaoran, tongping) attempted to upstream vAttention into vLLM itself (cites vLLM PRs/issues directly), and vAttention is implemented as a drop-in replacement inside vLLM's codebase (github.com/microsoft/vattention) rather than a from-scratch engine — this is an intra-ecosystem architecture fight, not a rival project competing for adoption. `review` (13960 words)
- [[vllm-router-v0120-routing-on-real-kv-cache-events-barrahomeorg]] — vLLM Router v0.12.0: Routing on Real KV Cache Events - barrahome.org — Named-practitioner (Alberto, bet0x/vllm-router fork maintainer) technical deep-dive blog, 5th in a series, on vLLM Router v0.12.0's new kv_aware routing policy that subscribes directly to vLLM's native ZMQ KV-cache-event stream (BlockStored/BlockRemoved msgpack frames on a PUB socket, default port 5557) instead of inferring cache state or polling an LMCache controller over HTTP (~200ms round trip). The router maintains a DashMap<block_hash, Vec<worker_url>> index, re-hashes block keys from token_ids with its own chained FNV-1a (not trusting vLLM's own hash, since different vLLM versions/configs can produce different hashes for the same tokens) so it works identically whether vLLM or LMCache published the event. Implements 'speculative insert' with a 2000ms TTL to close the race window between a routing decision and the corresponding BlockStored event landing, preventing duplicate cold misses on bursty concurrent requests with the same prefix. Also introduces rendezvous/HRW hashing as an alternative to ring-based consistent hashing, claiming ~49% lower coefficient of variation at 512 sessions/3 workers and that only sessions mapped to a removed worker get redistributed (validated by a contract test moving 'at most a handful of 300 test sessions'). Documents v0.11.0's Unix domain socket backend support (unix:///path.sock instead of http://host:port) for same-host deployments, explicitly independent of Kubernetes networking, though PD modes reject UDS at startup (KV transfer needs a real host:port). Known limitation as of v0.12.0: ignores the LMCache 'medium' field (GPU/CPU/disk offload tier) so it overestimates benefit when the matched prefix lives off GPU HBM; medium-aware scoring is slated for v0.13. Explicitly scopes the router as consumer-only of KV events, not an orchestrator: 'it receives a request, decides which worker handles it, and forwards the traffic.' Fork lives at github.com/bet0x/vllm-router, independent from vllm-project/router; source citations for kv_aware architecture are the fork's own CHANGELOG.md and docs/kv-events-routing.md. `evergreen` (2367 words)
- [[vllm-router-a-high-performance-and-prefilldecode-aware-load-balancer-for-large-s]] — vLLM Router: A High-Performance and Prefill/Decode Aware Load Balancer for Large-scale Serving | vLLM Blog — vLLM project's own Dec 2025 blog announcing the standalone vLLM Router (github.com/vllm-project/router), a Rust load balancer forked from the SGLang model gateway and simplified for vLLM. Runs 'either in a K8s or a bare metal GPU cluster' — K8s service discovery via label selectors is one mode, not a requirement. Supports consistent hashing (session/KV affinity), power-of-two, round robin/random policies, and native P/D disaggregation with worker discovery via NIXL or NCCL+ZMQ. Adversarial benchmark claims (methodology disclosed only at a high level, no raw data/repro harness given): on Llama 3.1 8B with 8 prefill/8 decode pods, vLLM Router throughput is 25% higher than llm-d and 100% higher than the K8s-native round-robin LB, with TTFT 1200ms faster than llm-d; on DeepSeek V3 (1 prefill TP8 + 1 decode TP8) throughput is close to llm-d and TTFT is 2000ms faster. The post explicitly excludes vLLM's built-in DP/EP coordinator from the comparison (citing a known throughput bug, vllm-project/vllm#24461) and thanks llm-d engineers 'for sharing llm-d expertise and receipts which unblocked performance optimizations' — i.e., the comparison was tuned with llm-d team input, not an independent third-party benchmark. `evergreen` (854 words)
- [[vllm-sessions-at-pytorch-conference-north-america-2026-pytorch]] — vLLM Sessions at PyTorch Conference North America 2026 - PyTorch — Primary source: PyTorch Foundation's own published session guide (Aug 28, 2026) for PyTorch Conference North America 2026 (San Jose, Oct 20-21), the source document the Futurum analyst piece in this batch summarizes secondhand.
